## 🆕 `p474` — `2026-10-27` — **🟢 `Gap 473-E`'s FOUR-PASS MIDDLE-EAST-AND-AFRICA DEMAND NIL IS FILLED, and the finding is a posture difference rather than a number: across Saudi Arabia, the UAE, Egypt, Nigeria, Kenya, Rwanda, Morocco and South Africa there are AI STRATEGIES AND NO BINDING AI STATUTE — so an MEA engagement is governed by procurement and policy, not by conformity assessment, which is the exact inverse of EMEA-Europe. 🔴 And the 2026 market figure now has FOUR mutually incompatible values from four vendors; all four are printed and none is averaged.**

**both are printed and the disagreement is named** rather than averaged. 🔴 Vendor-authored
statistics roundups and market-research vendor pages are marked as such; they are not primary
research and `Gap 455-E` grades them as structurally relayed.

### 🟢 `Gap 473-D` honoured by measurement, not by assumption

### 🔴 The 2026 market size — four vendors, four numbers, no average

| stated 2026 value | implied growth | source class |
|---|---|---|
| **≈$3.7 B** | ~21 % CAGR to 2033 | market-research vendor |
| **≈$10.6 B** | 40.9 % CAGR (from $7.52 B in 2025) | market-research vendor |
| **≈$11.4 B** | 25.9 % CAGR 2026→2033 | market-research vendor |
| **$6.4 B–$11.4 B** (range carried since `p467`) | — | this base's standing range |

🔴 **The spread is 2.9× on the same year.** 🔵 A $3.7 B and a $10.6 B estimate for 2026 cannot both
be measuring the same thing, and none of the four publishes its segment boundary — so the number is
unusable as a sizing input and is carried only to show the disagreement. 🟢 **The standing range
`$6.4 B–$11.4 B` is unchanged**, and the new $3.7 B anchor is recorded as a **low outlier**, not
folded in.

🔴 **`Gap 466-F` holds and the retired figure came back a third time.** The Multistate tracker's
**"134 bills across 31 states"** reappeared as this pass's lead North America statistic and **stays
retired**. 🟢 This pass can now say *why* the trackers disagree: ExcelinEd counts **≈100 K-12-specific**
bills against Multistate's 134 **all-education** bills, so the two are measuring different scopes —
not contradicting each other. 🔵 Scope, not arithmetic, explains part of `p455`'s 2.6× spread.

## Opportunities by region

🔵 One canonical block, five children, written in the pass that measured the signals above.

### North America

🟢 **The lead opportunity is the compliance artefact, not the tutor.** Idaho SB 1227 is **enacted**
with AI-literacy standards, educator training and a bar on AI replacing teachers; Oklahoma and
Maryland require human oversight and forbid AI as the main basis for high-stakes student decisions;
Vermont would make ed-tech vendors **certify** privacy compliance. 🔵 Every one of those is a
*demonstrability* requirement, and the asset that satisfies it is an auditable record of what the
AI did to each learner — pattern **`P474-1`** in [`compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md),
built on an MIT/Apache xAPI spine.
🔴 **The risk to price in is the federal/state collision**: a December 2025 order pushes to limit
state AI rules while 31 states legislate. 🟢 **The hedge is to build to the strictest state rule and
make the evidence exportable** — a district-level deliverable survives either outcome; a product
betting on federal pre-emption does not.
🟢 **Supply match:** [EduSafeBench](https://github.com/kaushikatla-cell/EduSafeBench) (MIT) is the
only evaluation asset on these shelves aimed at a US curriculum programme (AP CSA/CSP).

### EMEA

🟢 **Europe: the Council of Europe is designing a European evaluation framework for educational
technology — and the open evaluation tier that would feed it is the weakest shelf in this KB.** That
is the single clearest supply/demand mismatch this base has measured. 🔵 An engagement that can
produce defensible evaluation evidence under the EU AI Act's high-risk duties, with instruments it
can actually license, is selling into a requirement that is being written right now. 🔴 Mind the
boundary: Open edX is **AGPL** (network trigger) and Sakai is **ECL-2.0**, whose patent grant this
pass measured as **conditional on the contributing institution's research-funding agreements** — so
"Apache-approved" does not cover Sakai.
🟢 **Türkiye is a named, un-worked entry point**:
[Atölye.Platform](https://github.com/PolyOS-Team/Atolye.Platform) is **MIT whole-work** and targets
**Pardus**, the state Linux distribution — a public-sector deployment baseline inherited rather than
argued for.
🟢 **MEA: the posture inverts.** Strategies and no binding statute means the gate is **procurement
and national strategy alignment**, not conformity assessment — faster to enter, and won on
credentials and local-language capability rather than on a CE-style file. 🔵 Tier the approach:
Gulf states (Saudi Arabia, the UAE, Qatar) fund large national programmes; Egypt, Morocco and
Jordan buy through public-private partnerships; lower-income markets are infrastructure-blocked, so
**offline-capable small-model tutoring** is the only honest offer there — and
[slm-writing-feedback-tutor-ptbr](https://github.com/RandMelville/slm-writing-feedback-tutor-ptbr)
proves the ≤3.8B-offline pattern, in the wrong language but with transferable method.
🔴 **Do not price MEA on local open-source ecosystem depth**: Arabic-language GitHub presence is
**8.5× thinner than Turkish across all domains** (`repos/trending.md`), so expect to bring the
stack and localise, not to compose a local one. 🟢 African-origin capability exists and is nameable:
[wassce-benchmark](https://github.com/acheamponge/wassce-benchmark) (MIT, West Africa) and
[overlooked-bench](https://github.com/kcelestinomaria/overlooked-bench) (AGPL-3.0, Kenya).

### APAC

🔴 **The honest position is that this base cannot yet place an APAC education engagement on
evidence.** Every regional query this pass returned enterprise-AI material; no education-specific
rule in force was found; no adoption rate for schools or universities was found. 🔵 That is a
declared gap, not a quiet one — and it is the same nil `p473` recorded, now reproduced on a second
query set.
🟡 **What can be acted on:** the region is called the fastest-growing at 24.8 % CAGR (vendor), the
**TCS–Pearson** alliance signals that the buyer may be the *employer* rather than the ministry, and
Australia is tightening AI governance and copyright — which makes **provenance of training and
assessment content** the live question there.
🟢 **Supply is real even where demand data is not**: [Eyedot](https://github.com/Simon-zj1/eyedot)
(MIT, China) does per-scoring-point grading with low-confidence human review — exactly the
"human in the loop on high-stakes decisions" shape North American statutes demand, built in APAC.
🔴 And `Gap 467-F`'s Chinese **NonCommercial** content concentration is still ungraded at breadth:
compose the agents, license the content elsewhere.

### LATAM

🟢 **This is the region where this base now has the most complete picture, and the opportunity is
the national-curriculum stack.** [bncc-dev](https://github.com/bncc-dev) gives Brazil's curriculum
as MIT open data (1,721 objectives), an **MCP server** that makes it callable from an agent, and a
**contamination-resistant hallucination benchmark** over it — curriculum, interface and evidence
from one organisation. 🔵 Nothing equivalent exists for any other country on these shelves, and
building it for Mexico, Colombia or Chile is a concrete, scoped offer.
🔴 **The licence map is the delivery risk, and it is now fully drawn.** Portabilis — Brazil's
largest free school software vendor — ships **i-educar GPL-2.0-or-later**, **i-diario AGPL-3.0-or-later**
and **pre-matricula-digital AGPL-3.0-or-later**, the last two with a payload **in Portuguese** that
every English-anchored licence reader returns as `UNSTATED`. 🟢 Two of three components carry the
**network-use** trigger, so the AGPL boundary — not the model choice — is the architecture decision:
pattern **`P474-2`**.
🔴 **And the public data is not licensed.** São Paulo's municipal education secretariat
([dados-educacao](https://github.com/prefeiturasp/dados-educacao), 57★) and **Paraguay's Ministry of
Education** ([mec-opendata](https://github.com/mecpy/mec-opendata)) both publish **UNGRANTED** at
all 14 probed paths. 🔵 Treat them as read-only reference; source licensed equivalents
(`bncc-dados`, `ResultadosUNAM---data`, `chile-hub`, all MIT).
🟢 **Spanish-speaking LATAM is not the adopter-only market `p473` described.** At the no-star floor
the Spanish token returns **20 % more** education repositories than Portuguese; what differs is
star accumulation (2.49×), and what the Spanish leg yields is **government and public-data tooling**
— Uruguay's [ElijoEstudiar](https://github.com/datauy/ElijoEstudiar), Mexico's UNAM admissions data,
Chile's public-data hub. 🔵 Regulatory divergence is a sales fact, not a blocker: Brazil pending
risk-based, Chile risk-based, **Argentina rejecting regulation** — three different pitches.

### Global

🟢 **The cross-region constant is that buyers are converging on evidence and the open supply cannot
license it.** The Council of Europe is drafting an evaluation framework, US states demand human
oversight and auditability, HolonIQ reports systems expecting proof of learning gains — and the
instruments that would supply the proof sit at 0–11★ with three of the best carrying
**`SPLIT_SCOPE`** grants whose corpora are not licensed to the user.
🔴 **So the highest-value global capability is boring and defensible: a licence-and-scope reading
practice.** This pass found a 1.03M-problem corpus whose own LICENSE retracts the grant an index
reports, a vendor whose AGPL hides behind Portuguese, and a permissive family (ECL-2.0) whose
patent grant depends on a university's funding agreements. 🟢 Any of the three, shipped unexamined,
is a client liability; catching them is a two-hour read with
[`family_lang.py`](../compose/code/p474-scope-and-language/family_lang.py).
🟢 **The clean build targets are the generated and whole-work-permissive assets**:
[scireason-forge](https://github.com/chenhz01/scireason-forge) (Apache-2.0, items generated so no
third-party corpus), [studentbench](https://github.com/Handshake-AI-Research/studentbench) (MIT),
[bncc-dados](https://github.com/bncc-dev/bncc-dados) (MIT) and the permissive xAPI/LRS tier
`Gap 467-I` opened.

### 🔵 The global picture — unchanged in substance, and still not a quotable number

| Figure | Source class | Value |
|---|---|---|
| AI in education, 2026 | HolonIQ (analyst) | **$12.3 B** |
| AI in education, 2024 → 2030 | statistics roundup | ~**$6 B** → ~**$32 B** |
| AI in education, by 2035 | market-research vendor | **$136.79 B** |
| North America share, 2025 | market-research vendor | **38.2 %** |
| North America share, 2026 | statistics roundup | **36 %**, **$3.68 B** |

| APAC, 2026 | one report | **$2.85 B**, 35.3 % CAGR |
| APAC CAGR | a second report | **44.20 %** |

🔴 **`p472`'s arithmetic refutation stands and this pass adds a second incoherence.** The same
roundup family that puts North America at 36 % of a ~$32 B 2030 global total also gives North
America *"$32 B by 2030"* — the global figure pasted onto a regional row. 🔴 And the newly-surfaced
**$136.79 B by 2035** cannot be reconciled with ~$32 B in 2030 at any CAGR the same sources quote: it
implies ~34 % sustained growth for five years *after* the forecast window the other figures close in.
🟢 **Operative guidance, unchanged: do not put a single market size in a client deck from this
shelf.** Quote direction (strong double-digit growth, APAC fastest) and quote **adoption and
governance counts**, which hold up across sources and are what actually gate a project.

### 🟢 Adoption and governance — the numbers that hold up

| Measure | Region | Value | Source class |
|---|---|---|---|
| Institutions using AI in ≥1 area | LATAM / Caribbean | **87 %** | UNESCO IESALC + UNU-IAS, 200 institutions, 19 countries |
| Institutions with a **formal AI strategy** | LATAM / Caribbean | 🔴 **26 %** | same study |
| Using AI in teaching and learning | LATAM / Caribbean | **74 %** | same study |
| K-12 teachers using AI tools (US) | North America | ~**60 %** | Gallup, via roundup |
| Teens using ChatGPT for schoolwork (US) | North America | **26 %** | Pew, via roundup |
| University students using AI | Global | **92 %** | one 2026 report |
| Students using AI across 16 countries | Global | **86 %** | a second report, different method |

🔴 **The 87 / 26 pair remains the most consequential number in this KB**, and the study's own
explanation is the reason: adoption is **driven by individual academics, researchers and students
rather than institution-wide strategy.** A 61-point gap between what is running and what is governed
is not a market-sizing argument — it is a scope of work.

deadline is unresolved.** A behavioural-monitoring or automated-assessment deployment in Vietnam is
**high-risk by name**, and the two candidate compliance dates are six months apart. 🔵 Recorded as
a disagreement to resolve from the Vietnamese official gazette, not averaged.

🔴 **`Gap 467-F` holds on the supply side and this pass sharpens it.** The APAC agent tier is open —
`HKUDS/DeepTutor` is Apache-2.0 and the largest education repository this base carries — while the
APAC **content** tier is closed: [`xiaoyang111222/cn-math-taxonomy`](https://github.com/xiaoyang111222/cn-math-taxonomy),
now **4,103★** (from 4,086 nine days after creation), is **CC BY-NC 4.0** `19,347 B` `41003d4a7474`,
re-read first-hand this pass. 🟢 **And the pass found its exact LATAM counterpart openly licensed** —
see below. **"Compose the APAC agents, license the content elsewhere"** is now a positioned
recommendation rather than a hypothesis.

### LATAM

🔵 **Shape: the strongest demand signal in this KB, a regulator still in committee, and — as of this
pass — supply that was there all along in another language.**

| signal | detail | source class |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 **The demand gap** | **87 %** of institutions using AI, **26 %** with a formal strategy, **74 %** using it in teaching | UNESCO IESALC + UNU-IAS, 19 countries |
| 🟡 **Brazil** | **PL 2338/2023**, EU-style risk-based, **still moving through Congress**; Brazil launched the **region's first AI regulatory sandbox** in 2025 | legal commentary |
| 🔴 **Mexico** | **no enacted AI-specific statute** as of early 2026. 2026's changes came through other law: copyright, labour and criminal amendments on **performer impersonation and deepfakes** | legal commentary |
| 🟡 **Mexico, state capacity** | at least **14 federal agencies** already running AI systems (audit, security, citizen services), per FOI-based reporting | journalism |

| 🔴 **Education-specific regulation** | one commentary states education systems are integrating AI *"often without oversight or guidelines"* — **unsourced to data**, recorded as a token | commentary |

🟢 **`Gap 467-E` is answered on the axis it named, and the answer reverses this file's standing LATAM
position.** The gap said visible LATAM open-source education supply was **1 row in 103 (1 %), and
archived**, and prescribed running the leg on the **language axis** rather than the English
vocabulary. 🟢 Run on Portuguese and Spanish tokens, it returns a working stack:

| what | repo | grant, read first-hand |
|---|---|---|
| School information system, **552 forks** | [portabilis/i-educar](https://github.com/portabilis/i-educar) | 🟡 GPL-2.0-**or-later**, 18,092 B |

🟢 **So the standing finding changes from "near-absent supply" to "supply invisible to the
instrument."** 🔴 **And it is asymmetric within LATAM:** the Portuguese token at ≥20★ returned 15
repositories including all of the above; the Spanish token at ≥20★ returned **8**, of which **one**
is education infrastructure. On this evidence the LATAM open-source education supply is
**Brazilian**, and that should be tested at a lower star floor before it is carried as standing.

🟢 **The regional contrast worth putting in front of a client.** The richest curriculum knowledge
graph in APAC is **NonCommercial**; Brazil's national curriculum is **MIT**, with a **CC BY 4.0**
benchmark over it. 🟢 **A LATAM engagement can ship the content layer that an APAC engagement
cannot** — the exact inverse of this file's standing assumption.

### Global

🔵 **The cross-regional opportunity this pass surfaces is the one nobody is selling: evidence.**

- 🟢 Every regional signal above is converging on the same demand — **1EdTech** calls 2026 the shift
  *from experimentation to governance*; the **OECD** recommends purpose-built over general-purpose;
  **HolonIQ** reports systems increasingly expecting *evidence that products improve learning
  quality, persistence, well-being or job-relevant skills*; **Oklahoma** would bar AI as the main
  basis for grading; the **EU AI Act** requires demonstrable human oversight on assessment.
- 🔴 **And the open-source tier that would supply that evidence is the least usable shelf in this
  KB**: 9 instruments, **44.4 % ungranted**, the flagship (ETH Zurich's `mathtutorbench`, EMNLP 2025
  Oral) granting nothing, and exactly **two** usable — one West-African at 4★ and one Brazilian at
  9★ (`agents/top.md`).
- 🟢 **That asymmetry is the commercial opening.** Capability is commoditised: `DeepTutor` is
  Apache-2.0 at 44k★. **Proof is not.** A studio that can show a regulator, a district or a
  ministry *how it measured* its tutor is selling the scarce half, and the apparatus to do it is
  permissive and named — `terracotta` (Apache-2.0) for the trial design, `lrsql`/`ralph` for the
  telemetry, `wassce-benchmark` and `bncc-benchmark` for the scored baselines. Wired up in
  `compose/patterns.md` `P473-3`.

## 🆕 `p472` — `2026-10-27` — **🔴 THE NORTH AMERICA MARKET FIGURES IN CIRCULATION ARE ARITHMETICALLY IMPOSSIBLE AS STATED: the same roundup puts NA at a 36 % share worth $3.68 B in 2026 and then has NA reaching "$32 B by 2030" — the identical figure it gives as the GLOBAL 2030 total. One of the two is misattributed, and a deck that quotes both is self-contradicting. 🟢 And the single most actionable number on this shelf is UNESCO's LAC pair: 87 % of institutions using AI against 26 % holding a strategy — a 61-point gap that IS the engagement.**

🔵 Every figure below is attributed to the source that stated it. Where sources disagree, **both are
printed and the disagreement is named** rather than averaged. 🔴 Vendor-authored statistics
roundups are marked as such; they are not primary research.

### 🔴 The global picture, and why no single market number should be quoted

| Figure | Source class | Value |
|---|---|---|
| AI in education, 2024 → 2030 | statistics roundup | ~**$6 B** (2024) → ~**$32 B** (2030) |
| AI in education, 2026 | HolonIQ (analyst) | **$12.3 B** |
| NA share, 2026 | statistics roundup | **36 %**, **$3.68 B** |
| Europe, 2026 → 2030 | statistics roundup | **$2.64 B** → ~**$8.0 B** |
| APAC, 2026 | one report | **$2.85 B**, **35.3 % CAGR** |
| APAC CAGR | a second report | **44.20 %** |

🔴 **Two independent incoherences, both published:**

1. **The NA figure cannot be both.** A region at 36 % share of a ~$32 B 2030 global total is
   ~$11.5 B, not $32 B. The roundup's *"$3.68 B in 2026 → $32 B by 2030"* for North America is the
   **global** trajectory pasted onto a regional row.
2. **$3.68 B at 36 % implies a 2026 global of ~$10.2 B**, which contradicts HolonIQ's **$12.3 B**
   for the same year by about 17 %.

🟢 **Operative guidance: do not put a single market size in a client deck from this shelf.** Quote
the *direction* (strong double-digit growth, APAC fastest) and quote **adoption and governance
counts**, which are consistent across sources and are what actually gate a project.

### 🟢 Adoption and governance — the numbers that hold up

| Measure | Region | Value | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Institutions using AI in ≥1 area | LATAM / Caribbean | **87 %** | UNESCO IESALC + UNU-IAS, 200 institutions, 19 countries |
| Institutions with a **formal AI strategy** | LATAM / Caribbean | 🔴 **26 %** | same study |
| Using AI in teaching and learning | LATAM / Caribbean | **74 %** | same study |
| K-12 teachers using AI, 2024-25 | North America (US) | ~**60 %** | statistics roundup |
| …of whom at least weekly | North America (US) | **32 %** | same |
| AI-in-education bills introduced, 2026 | North America (US) | **134 bills across 31 states** | legislative tracker |

🔴 **The 87 / 26 pair is the most consequential number on this shelf.** Adoption has already
happened without governance, and the study states adoption is **driven by individual academics,
researchers and students rather than institution-wide strategy**. That is a 61-point gap between
what is running and what is governed.

## Opportunities by region — 🔴 SUPERSEDED by `p473` (consolidated into the single current block above; content retained here and in `git log`)

### North America

🔵 **Regulatory posture — restriction, with federal literacy funding moving the other way.**

- **Classroom restriction is now real, not proposed.** New York City runs a **one-year moratorium on
  student-facing AI through eighth grade**, and it is being promoted as a template to other
  districts (Nebraska districts named).
- **Named statutes and bills:** **California AB 1159** restricts training AI models on student
  data; **Idaho SB 1227** sets privacy requirements for school AI tools; **Oklahoma** and
  **Maryland** require human oversight and **bar AI from high-stakes decisions about students**;
  **Georgia** and **Mississippi** add computer-science credits including AI instruction.
- **Federal:** the **K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act (H.R. 8747)** advanced out of the House
  Education and Workforce Committee in **July 2026**, largely along party lines, and would allow
  ESEA funds for AI curriculum and literacy. A White House executive order on AI literacy and a
  **200+ company** *Pledge to America's Youth* (Google, OpenAI, Microsoft, NVIDIA, Salesforce named)
  precede it.
- **Canada:** national **"AI for All"** strategy launched **4 June 2026** with an AI-literacy
  commitment and **no AI statute**, no legislation date confirmed.
- **Mexico:** bills pending, **no regulator to enforce them**.
- **Institutional players:** the **AFT** launched a **National Academy for AI Instruction** with the
  UFT, Microsoft, OpenAI and Anthropic. State agencies are issuing guidance (North Carolina DPI's
  K-12 AI guidance named).

🟢 **Opportunity.** The Oklahoma/Maryland human-oversight requirement and the NYC age-gating
moratorium together define a **buildable product shape**: AI that produces **advisory output with a
recorded human sign-off**, age-gated, and provably not making the decision. Globant's permissive
stack for this is **Sakai (ECL-2.0) or OpenOLAT (Apache-2.0)** as the gradebook of record with
**DeepTutor (Apache-2.0)** confined to advisory generation — recipe `P4`. The US incumbent is
**Canvas (AGPL-3.0)**, so the licence-boundary design of `P2` is a prerequisite, not an extra.
🔴 Note the local repo risk: the two **ungranted** rows on `agents/top.md` are both NA-origin
(`Mr.-Ranedeer`, `ai-ethos`).

### EMEA

🔵 **Regulatory posture — the strictest classification in the world, with a disputed timetable.**

- **The EU AI Act classes AI used for admissions, student evaluation and exam scoring as
  high-risk**, and it reaches **non-EU vendors** whose tools affect EU students.
- 🔴 **The timetable is in dispute across sources read this pass.** A secondary source states a
  **"Digital Omnibus"** amendment took effect **late July 2026**, pushing high-risk obligations to
  **December 2027** and **August 2028**. The European Commission's own milestone page gives
  different dates. **Both are recorded; neither is treated as settled.** Any client commitment must
  be checked against the official text, not against this shelf.
- **Soft law:** the Commission's **ethical guidelines for educators on AI and data** (first
  published 2022) remain the main reference for schools. **Erasmus+** funds AI-in-education
  projects. The **OECD 2026 Digital Education Outlook** argues for **purpose-built educational AI
  over general-purpose tools** — an analyst position that directly favours a studio build over a
  ChatGPT rollout.
- **Leaders named:** **Finland, Estonia, the Netherlands** in K-12 AI integration.
- **Middle East:** the **UAE made AI a compulsory school subject from the 2025-26 year** — the only
  hard Middle East finding this pass produced, and it surfaced via the APAC search, not the EMEA
  one.

🔴 **Declared EMEA gap, explicitly.** The EMEA search returned **nothing** on Gulf-state national AI
education programmes beyond the UAE subject mandate, **nothing** on Saudi initiatives, and
**nothing** on African government education-AI policy. No African or Middle East-origin education
repository surfaced at any star floor. 🔵 **This is an informed gap, not coverage**: EMEA on this
shelf is, in practice, **Europe plus one UAE data point**, and should be sold as such.

🟢 **Opportunity.** The high-risk classification makes **compliance the deliverable**, not a
constraint on it: an EU engagement buys an auditable assessment pipeline. The permissive EMEA-native
core is **OpenOLAT (Apache-2.0)** — QTI assessment and certification are first-class in it — with
**Academico (MIT)** as the SIS and **get-it (Apache-2.0, Milan-origin)** as a reference for the
study loop. Recipe `P4`. Incumbents to expect: **ILIAS** and **Chamilo** (both GPL-3.0),
**Tutor/Open edX** (AGPL-3.0, France-origin).

### APAC

🔵 **Regulatory posture — the widest divergence of any region, and the only hard dated education
obligation on this shelf.**

- **South Korea:** the **AI Basic Act took effect 22 January 2026** — described as the world's
  second comprehensive AI law after the EU's. Government reportedly invested **~$740 M (2024-26)**
  training teachers on AI tools.
- **Vietnam:** a comprehensive AI law **lists education among its high-risk sectors**, naming
  **automated assessment and behavioural monitoring**. 🔴 **Sources disagree on timing**: one gives
  compliance due **1 March 2027** with six extra months for education systems already running
  before **15 August 2026**; another gives the law in force **1 March 2026** with transition to
  **1 September 2027** for health, education and finance. 🟢 `p471` recorded the instrument as
  **Decision No. 33/2026/QD-TTg** with a **1 March 2027** compliance date and a **15 August 2026**
  grace cut-off — **consistent with the first reading**, which this pass's independent search
  corroborates.
- **Japan:** voluntary guidelines. **Singapore:** voluntary testing toolkit. **Australia:** still
  drafting. **India:** innovation-first, no statute. **China:** the most restrictive framework —
  mandatory registration and penalties.
- **Sentiment:** the **Ipsos Education Monitor 2026** found **lower support for banning AI in
  schools across Asian markets** than in Australia and New Zealand, which show **higher** support
  for a ban. 🔵 A single regional strategy will not fit both halves of APAC.
- **Sovereign models in play:** **Sarvam AI** (India), **SEA-LION** (Singapore), **HyperCLOVA X**
  (South Korea), **Sarashina** (Japan).
- 🟢 **Build evidence, not just policy:** APAC **leads the open-source education build**.
  **DeepTutor** (HKU, Apache-2.0, 44,177★) is the largest education repository this base carries;
  `ai-shifu` (Apache-2.0), `ChatTutor` (AGPL-3.0), `lineage-skill` (Apache-2.0), `frappe/lms`
  (AGPL-3.0, India) and `cn-math-taxonomy` (CC BY-NC) are all APAC-origin.

🟢 **Opportunity.** Two distinct sales: (1) **multi-jurisdiction compliance architecture** — Korea's
Act, Vietnam's high-risk classification and China's registration regime cannot be served by one
configuration, so the deliverable is a policy-parameterised deployment; (2) **sovereign-model
integration** — four national models exist and the tutoring layer above them is open and permissive
(DeepTutor + LightRAG, both buildable). 🔴 The ready-made Chinese curriculum graph
(`cn-math-taxonomy`) is **NonCommercial** — see `P5` for the licit substitute.

### LATAM

🔵 **Regulatory posture — policy ahead of capability, and adoption ahead of both.**

- **UNESCO IESALC + UNU-IAS**, 200 institutions across 19 countries: **87 % use AI in at least one
  area, 74 % in teaching and learning, only 26 % have a formal AI strategy**, with adoption driven
  by individuals rather than institutions.
- **UNESCO launched the Observatory on Artificial Intelligence in Education for Latin America and
  the Caribbean on 14 April**, at **ECLAC in Santiago**, during the 2026 Forum of the Countries of
  LAC on Sustainable Development. 🟢 A regional benchmark instrument that did not exist before.
- **Instruments by country:** **Uruguay** became the **first in LATAM to sign the Council of Europe
  Framework Convention on AI** (2025). **Peru**'s framework includes **regulatory sandboxes** and a
  phased schedule. **Mexico**: federal AI bill in the Senate (2024) proposing an oversight
  authority. **Colombia**: national AI policy via **CONPES 4144** (February 2025) rather than a law.
- **Capacity:** UNESCO has trained **60 officials across Ecuador and Chile** in AI ethics and
  regulation.
- **Constraint:** the **Latin American AI Index (ILIA) 2025**, 3rd edition, 20 economies, finds
  school-level AI education and postgraduate programmes **growing but still exceptions**, and names
  the binding obstacles as **computing infrastructure, business adoption, and converting policy
  commitments into programmes**. Intelligent CIO (23 October 2026) reports **widening** LATAM AI
  inequality on compute and adoption.

🟢 **Opportunity — and it is a different sale from the other three regions.** With 87 % adoption and
26 % strategy, the first deliverable is **not** a tutor: it is an **AI governance and strategy
assessment** benchmarked against the new UNESCO LAC Observatory and ILIA 2025, which converts
shadow adoption into an institutional programme. The build that follows must respect the compute
finding: **Kolibri (MIT) is offline-first** and is the correct delivery core where connectivity is
the constraint; **ai-shifu (Apache-2.0)** gives a Spanish/Portuguese conversational teaching loop;
the incumbent to expect in public education is **Chamilo (GPL-3.0)**, which has a genuine LATAM
install base. Recipe `P6`.

🔴 **Declared LATAM build gap.** The only LATAM-origin open-source education repository surfaced in
this window — [menthorlabs/menthor](https://github.com/menthorlabs/menthor), Brazil, Apache-2.0,
582★ — is **archived and read-only**. 🔵 There is **no live LATAM-origin education-AI project** on
this shelf. Stated as a gap, because silence here would read exactly like coverage: the region's
policy apparatus is maturing faster than its open-source capability, which is itself the argument
for a studio engagement rather than a tool selection.

## 🆕 `p471` — **🔴 `Gap 470-E` IS REFUTED ON ITS OWN TERMS: `p470` predicted the unchanged regional template would keep returning a 90–100 % off-class rate in two of four regions, and the EMEA leg it measured at 100 % off-class came back this pass at 9 of 9 ON-class, same template, same words. 🟢 And APAC gains the hard dated obligation three passes have asked for and none could name: Vietnam's **Decision No. 33/2026/QD-TTg** classes education AI as high-risk, with a compliance date of **1 March 2027** and a named grace cut-off of **15 August 2026**.**

🔵 Gate not attested (`Gap 446-A`); checks re-implemented in-session and green. `region` values below
are the closed vocabulary only.

### 🔴 Channel integrity, stated before the content

🔵 **`Gap 470-D` is NOT closed, for the second consecutive pass, and the reason is unchanged.** Every
EU primary channel is still unreachable from this host — `eur-lex.europa.eu`,
`education.ec.europa.eu`, `digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu` and `artificialintelligenceact.eu` all
return **HTTP 000 / DNS failure** on `curl`. 🔴 The second and third of those were *returned as

regulation block below is therefore relayed secondary reporting, not first-hand text**, and is marked

### 🔴 `Gap 470-E` measured, and refuted

🔵 `p470` registered that the prescribed leg template — `AI {industry} {region} {year} adoption
regulation players` — is "enterprise-analyst vocabulary" that collides with CIO content, measured
EMEA at **0 on-class of 10**, and predicted a continuing 90–100 % off-class rate in two of four
regions unless the template named an institution type. Both templates were run this pass:

| leg (prescribed template, unchanged) | links | on-class for education | off-class rate |
|---|---|---|---|
| North America | 9 | **9** | 🟢 0 % |
| EMEA | 9 | **9** | 🟢 **0 %** — `p470` measured **100 %** |
| APAC | 9 | 2 | 🔴 78 % |
| LATAM | 10 | **10** | 🟢 0 % |

🔴 **The EMEA result inverts completely on an unchanged query.** The leg returned the Commission's
own education pages, an EU-AI-Act-for-education guide, a global regulatory bulletin and an education
ERP vendor's deployment survey — no CompTIA outlook, no Workday study, no CPaaS note. 🟢 So
`Gap 470-E`'s **mechanism** (enterprise-register collision) is real and observed, but its
**prediction** — that the rate is a stable property of the template — is wrong. The off-class rate
is **volatile between passes on identical input**, which means a single pass's off-class measurement
cannot license a template change. 🔵 Registered as **`Gap 471-F`**: an off-class rate must be
measured on at least two passes before it is attributed to the query rather than to the index.

🟡 **The corrected template was run too, and it has its own defect.** Naming institution types
(`schools universities ministry of education curriculum`) returned on-class education material in
both regions — and **leaked across regions**: the *EMEA* leg's top result was South Korea's
university AI programme, and the *APAC* leg returned the UAE. 🔴 The institution-type template fixes
class and loses geography, so it is a complement to the prescribed leg, not a replacement.

### 🟢 EMEA regulation — the instrument, its dates, and one date this base has not carried

🔵 Relayed, not first-hand (see channel integrity above). `p470` established **Regulation (EU)
2026/1744**, OJ of 24 July 2026, in force 27 July 2026. This pass's leg corroborates it
independently and adds a second date:

| item | date | status |
|---|---|---|
| Regulation (EU) 2026/1744 applies | **27 July 2026** | 🟢 corroborated, second independent channel |
| High-risk duties, **stand-alone Annex III** systems — where education sits | **2 December 2027** | 🟢 corroborated; confirms `p469`/`p470` |
| 🔴 High-risk duties, AI **embedded in regulated products** | **2 August 2028** | 🔴 **NEW to this base** — no prior pass carried it |
| Prohibition on non-consensual intimate imagery / CSAM | **2 December 2026** | 🟢 `p470`'s five-week item, now **five weeks out** |

🔴 **Why the 2 August 2028 date matters and is not a footnote.** Education AI does not only ship as
stand-alone software. An AI capability embedded in a regulated product — an exam-hall biometric
device, a medical or assistive-technology device used in a special-education setting, safety
equipment in a vocational workshop — sits on the **2028** track, not the 2027 one. A studio planning
one compliance programme against "December 2027" will mis-schedule that class of work by eight
months in either direction.

🟢 **The Annex III education scope, as relayed.** Education AI is high-risk where it evaluates
learning outcomes, assigns learners to programmes or institutions, detects prohibited behaviour
during assessment, or influences access to qualifications. The obligations named are a **conformity
assessment before placing on the market or putting into service**, and **qualified human staff able
to monitor outputs, intervene, override a decision and shut the system down**. 🔵 Against
`Gap 470-D`'s question — did 2026/1744 change the education entry itself, or only its date? — the
relayed wording is **substantively the same entry with a later date**. That is a partial answer from
a secondary channel and `Gap 470-D` stays open for a first-hand read.

### 🟢 APAC — the dated obligation, and the curriculum mandates behind it

🔵 `p468` asked for hard dated obligations in every region and APAC has been the thin leg since.
This pass it is the strongest:

| jurisdiction | instrument | what it does | dates |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Vietnam** | 🟢 **Decision No. 33/2026/QD-TTg** | Names high-risk AI in six sectors, **education among them** — automated assessment and behavioural monitoring. Classed high-risk **only where the output is the sole basis for a decision without meaningful human review** | 🔴 Compliance by **1 March 2027**; six-month grace for education systems already running before **15 August 2026**. Sources disagree on whether the parent law took effect 1 March 2026 — flagged, not resolved |
| **China** | MoE **"AI Plus Education"** action plan | Dedicated AI courses in primary and secondary schools; university curriculum and teacher-certification components per a secondary summary | Announced **10 April 2026**, full implementation by **2030** |
| **South Korea** | MoE *2026 University AI Basic Curriculum Development Support Project* | **20 universities selected from 80 applicants**; AI literacy for students in every major, curriculum to be shared sector-wide | Selections reported **April 2026** |
| **India** | CBSE circular **Acad-15/2026** | Computational Thinking & AI curriculum, Classes III–VIII | From the **2026–27** session |
| **Japan** | 🟡 unsettled | A national mandate for AI digital textbooks was reportedly **removed by statute** and reclassified as optional material | 🟡 status contested across sources; do not plan against it |

🔴 **The human-review carve-out is the commercially load-bearing clause.** Vietnam's rule bites only
where the system's output is the **sole** basis for a decision. A design that routes every
assessment or placement output through a reviewing teacher is outside the high-risk tier by
construction — the same escape the EU's Annex III human-oversight duty implies, arrived at
independently. **That is one architecture that satisfies both regimes**, and it is the single most
transferable design constraint on this file.

🔴 **One claim actively refuted, recorded so it is not re-relayed.** A widely-repeated assertion that
Singapore added AI to a *primary-level computer science* subject is false — there is no primary
computer-science subject in Singapore. A companion claim about AI teacher training by 2026 is
unsupported. Both were returned by this pass's legs and are rejected.

### 🟢 EMEA — the curriculum mandates the prescribed leg missed and the corrected leg found

| jurisdiction | what | dates |
|---|---|---|
| **UAE** | Ministry of Education made AI a **compulsory subject, KG–Grade 12**, 7 core areas, **no written exams** | Cabinet decision May 2025; from **2025–26** in public schools; extended to MoE-curriculum private schools reported from **August 2026** (secondary source) |
| **Qatar** | Ministry of Education and Higher Education adding **AI and data science** across primary, preparatory and secondary | **2026–27** school year |

🔵 Both are Gulf-state mandates, both are EMEA, and both are **procurement events with a date
attached** — a national curriculum that must be delivered from a fixed academic year is a
teacher-training, content-production and assessment-tooling pipeline, not a policy statement.

anywhere measured**. 🔵 This confirms `p468`'s governance reading with teacher-level evidence rather
than institutional self-report, and it is now double-sourced across two instruments.

🔵 The demand signal on this file has been consistent for four passes: the region-level gap is

## Opportunities by region — 🔴 SUPERSEDED by `p473` (consolidated into the single current block above; content retained here and in `git log`)

### North America

- 🟢 **Human-oversight evidence tooling for high-stakes decisions.** Oklahoma and Maryland bar AI
  from high-stakes student decisions and require human oversight; the EU and Vietnam converge on the
  same clause. The deliverable is an audit trail proving a human reviewed, could override, and did
  — reusable in all three regimes. 🔵 Compose on `TREMA-UNH/rubric-grading-workbench` (MIT, 15★,
  academic provenance) for the evaluation half.
- 🟢 **AI-literacy curriculum delivery, funded.** H.R. 8747 would make federal funds available for
  AI-literacy programmes; 134 bills across 31 states make the compliance surface state-specific.
  A per-state policy matrix is a sellable artefact before any software ships.
- 🟡 **Age-gated deployment.** New York City's moratorium through Grade 8 means any K-12 product
  sold into a US district needs a configurable age floor as a first-class feature, not a setting.

### EMEA

- 🔴 **Two compliance tracks, not one — and this is the region's most mis-scheduled work.**
  Stand-alone education AI is due **2 December 2027**; AI embedded in regulated products is due
  **2 August 2028**. Any EMEA engagement touching exam-hall hardware, assistive technology or
  vocational safety equipment is on the later track. Classify the estate by track first.
- 🔴 **The 2 December 2026 prohibition is five weeks out and has no conformity route.** Any
  image- or video-generating capability shipped into an EMEA education estate must prevent
  non-consensual intimate imagery and CSAM from that date. Prohibitions cannot be certified around:
  the capability is prevented or it is unlawful. 🔵 Audit every generative feature in an EMEA
  education deliverable now.
- 🟢 **Gulf curriculum delivery, with dates.** UAE (KG–12 compulsory, 7 areas, no written exams,
  private schools from August 2026) and Qatar (AI + data science across three levels, 2026–27) are
  dated national mandates — teacher training, content production and assessment tooling at national
  scale.
- 🟡 **Conformity-assessment packaging.** The Annex III duties name a pre-market conformity
  assessment and qualified human oversight. For vendors selling into EMEA education that is a
  documentation product, and it is billable ahead of the 2027 date.

### APAC

- 🟢 **Design to Vietnam's human-review carve-out and the EU's oversight duty at once.**
  Decision 33/2026/QD-TTg classes education AI high-risk **only where the output is the sole basis
  for a decision**. An architecture that routes every assessment and placement output through a
  reviewing teacher is outside the high-risk tier in Vietnam **and** satisfies EMEA's oversight
  obligation. One design, two regimes — build it once.
- 🟢 **Dated delivery pipelines.** Vietnam **1 March 2027** (grace for systems live before
  **15 August 2026**); India CBSE Classes III–VIII from **2026–27**; China's MoE plan to **2030**;
  South Korea's 20 selected universities sharing curriculum sector-wide. Each is a procurement
  window with a date on it.
- 🟡 **Do not plan against Japan.** The digital-textbook mandate is reported removed by statute and
  reclassified as optional; the sources conflict. Treat Japan as unsettled until a primary source
  is readable.

### LATAM

- 🔴 **The governance gap is the engagement, and it has no open-source supply.** Teacher AI use runs
  16–20 points above the OECD average (Brazil 56 %, Chile 55 %, Colombia 53 %, Costa Rica 52 %)
  while **26 %** of institutions have a formal AI strategy. The licence-filtered governance leg
  returns 61 repositories with a **16★ ceiling**. 🟢 This is the clearest **own-IP, build-from-scratch**
  opportunity on this file: an institutional AI-governance product — policy templates, usage
  disclosure, assessment-integrity rules, an audit trail — with no credible OSS starting point to be
  undercut by.
- 🟢 **Practitioner-first, not institution-first.** Teachers are already using AI at rates above the
  OECD mean. The sellable entry point is tooling that makes existing teacher practice auditable,
  not a platform that asks institutions to adopt something new.
- 🟢 **Rubric feedback, now with granted supply.** `ChantillyAn/homework-grader` (MIT, 7★, CoT
  scoring + bias mitigation) and `TREMA-UNH/rubric-grading-workbench` (MIT, 15★) are both permissive
  and both read first-hand this pass. 🟡 Both are small and single-maintainer — fork-and-own, do not
  take a dependency.
- 🟡 **Brazil and Chile are pre-statute.** PL 2.338/2023 is still in the Chamber of Deputies and
  Chile's bill is a bill. Sell against the **risk-based grammar** both share rather than against
  specific article numbers that can still move.

### Global

- 🟢 **Subtree licence audit as a billable deliverable.** Measured this pass: **33 of 229**
  education repositories (14.4 %) carry a grant below their root, rising to **66.7 %** above 2 000
  paths. Every major LMS is in that band — 149 licence payloads inside Moodle, 148 inside Open edX,
  64 inside ILIAS, 56 inside Canvas. 🔴 A client who believes "the platform is GPL, we checked the
  root" has not checked it. The instrument is two commands and the finding class includes
  **proprietary modules inside otherwise-open repositories** (ARSnova's `formatting/`).
- 🟢 **One oversight architecture clears three regimes.** EMEA Annex III human oversight, Vietnam's
  sole-basis carve-out, and US state rules barring AI from high-stakes student decisions all reduce
  to the same requirement: a reviewable human decision point with override and shutdown. Build it
  as a reusable component, not per engagement.

🟢 **But the gap closes anyway, through a channel `p469` did not consider.** The *search* channel

🔴 **The five-week item is the one to act on, and it is new to this base.** The non-consensual
intimate imagery and CSAM prohibition applies from **2 December 2026**. Schools and universities are
a primary venue for exactly that harm — peer deepfake harassment of pupils and staff — and any
image- or video-generating capability a studio ships into an education estate in EMEA is inside that
prohibition from that date. It is a **prohibition**, not a high-risk duty, so no postponement
applies and no conformity route exists: the capability is either prevented or it is unlawful.

🟡 **What is still not closed, stated plainly.** The Annex III wording itself has **not** been read

cheapest remaining closure is to read Article 113 as amended, in the consolidated text, from any

### 🔴 The prescribed regional legs are OFF-CLASS, and this pass measured it

🔵 This file has recorded regional legs returning "material already carried" for four passes. That
diagnosis was too generous. Graded by whether the returned source is **about education at all**:

| prescribed leg | links returned | education-specific | off-class rate |
|---|---|---|---|
| `AI education North America 2026 adoption regulation players` | 9 | **8** | 11 % |
| `AI education LATAM 2026 adoption regulation players` | 9 | **3** | 67 % |
| `AI education APAC 2026 adoption regulation players` | 10 | **1** | **90 %** |
| `AI education EMEA 2026 adoption regulation players` | 10 | **0** | 🔴 **100 %** |

🔴 **The EMEA leg returned ten links and not one was about education.** It returned enterprise-IT
analyst coverage: a CompTIA IT-strategy outlook (five times, in five locales), a 2023 Workday
AI-adoption study, a CPaaS research note, a customer-contact webinar. The APAC leg returned the same
register — Forrester CEO adoption rates, Boomi tech priorities, board governance gaps.

🟢 **The mechanism, and it is actionable.** `adoption regulation players` is **enterprise-analyst
vocabulary**. For North America the education-market research industry publishes against those words
too, so the leg works. For EMEA and APAC it collides with a far larger enterprise-IT content
industry and education loses. This is `p468`'s vocabulary finding on a **new axis**: not the
*language* of the query but its **register**. 🟢 Registered as **`Gap 470-E`**: the regional legs
must name an education institution type — *schools, universities, ministry of education, curriculum*
— or they will keep returning CIO content. An unchanged template will keep producing a 90–100 %
off-class rate in two of four regions, and reporting it as saturation.

## 🆕 `p469` — **🔴 THE SINGLE MOST CONSEQUENTIAL DATE IN EDUCATION AI JUST MOVED, AND IT MOVED IN THE DIRECTION NOBODY PRICED: the EU AI Act's high-risk obligations for education — admissions decisions and learner assessment — are reported POSTPONED from 2 August 2026 to 2 DECEMBER 2027 by the AI omnibus. 🟢 That is sixteen extra months of build runway in EMEA. 🔴 And this pass could not reach the primary source to confirm it, which is stated here rather than hidden.**

🔵 Market-size estimates on this shelf's prior passes conflict by an order of magnitude across
sources ($32 B by 2030 vs $136.79 B by 2035 on the same metric). This pass publishes **regulation
and adoption with dates and institutions** — which is auditable — and refuses to add another
unverifiable headline number to the pile.

### 🔴 Channel integrity, stated before the content

| channel | probe | result |
|---|---|---|
| `digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu` (EU primary source) | page fetch | 🔴 **DNS `ENOTFOUND`** from this host — unreachable |
| `api.github.com` direct | `/repos/{owner}/{repo}` | 🔴 **403**, unchanged for 469 passes |
| session GitHub tool channel | `stargazers_count` | 🟢 reachable; every star figure this pass |
| `raw.githubusercontent.com` | licence payloads | 🟢 reachable; every grant this pass |

🔴 **So the AI Act dates below are SECONDARY-SOURCED, and the sources disagree with each other.**
One set of guides puts education's Annex III obligations at **2 December 2027**; another still
reads **2 August 2026**. The December-2027 date appears in the more recent and more specific
sources, and the mechanism they name — the **"AI omnibus"** amendments, proposed November 2025,
**adopted June 2026**, **in force 27 July 2026** — is consistent across them. 🟡 Published as the
best available reading, flagged as unconfirmed at source. 🟢 Registered as **`Gap 469-E`**: the
cheapest closure available to the next pass is to reach EUR-Lex or the Official Journal from a
channel that resolves, and either confirm 2 December 2027 or correct it. An EMEA engagement
scoped on the wrong date is scoped by sixteen months.

### 🟢 Regulation, by region, with dates

**EMEA.** AI Act enforcement by the AI Office and national authorities **began 2 August 2026**. The
**AI literacy duty has applied since 2 February 2025** to every organisation that uses an AI
system — which means **every school**, already, with no grace period. Education sits in **Annex
III (high-risk)** for admissions decisions and learner assessment; those obligations are reported
moved to **2 December 2027**, with AI embedded in regulated products at **2 August 2028**. Two
Commission reports dated **22 September 2026** describe generative AI as a mainstream tool in EU
education and record that **nearly two-thirds of European education systems now have an AI
strategy, guidance or policy framework**. Schools are mostly in pilot-and-pre-compliance mode;
most deployments call OpenAI or Anthropic APIs, while districts under strict data-residency rules
self-host open-weight models. A school using a commercial AI tool to assess student progress is a
**deployer** under the Act, not a bystander.

**North America.** 🔴 No federal statute; the action is state-level. A 2026 tracker counts **134
AI-in-education bills across 31 states**. Named examples: **California AB 1159** bars using student
data to train AI models; **Idaho SB 1227** requires privacy protections for school AI tools;
**Oklahoma and Maryland** require human oversight and bar AI from high-stakes decisions about
students. Federally, the **K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026 (H.R. 8747)** advanced out of
committee **21 July 2026**, largely along party lines, and would let schools spend federal funds on
AI literacy. **New York City** holds a **one-year moratorium on student-facing AI through eighth
grade**. **Canada:** no federal AI statute; the **"AI for All" strategy (June 2026)** commits to a
national AI-literacy push with no legislative date. **Mexico:** bills exist, no regulator to
enforce them.

**APAC.** 🔴 The most divergent region on earth for this. **South Korea's AI Basic Act is in force
as of 22 January 2026**, covering high-impact systems with risk-management and disclosure duties.
**Vietnam** is the one to read closely: a 2026 decision names **education among six high-risk
sectors**, explicitly including **automated assessment and behavioural monitoring**, high-risk
**only where AI output is the sole basis for a decision without meaningful human review** —
compliance due **1 March 2027**, with a **six-month grace period for education systems already
operating before 15 August 2026**. **Japan and Singapore** remain voluntary: guidelines and a
testing toolkit, not binding rules. **Australia** is still drafting and considering mandatory
guardrails for high-risk AI. **China** has the strictest regime, with mandatory registration and
penalties. Ipsos' 2026 Education Monitor puts support for banning AI in schools **lowest in
Indonesia (23 %)** and **highest in South Korea (31 %)** among Asian markets surveyed, with
**Australia and New Zealand higher still**.

**LATAM.** 🟢 Adoption runs ahead of governance, and the numbers are the strongest on this shelf.
**TALIS 2024**: **56 % of secondary teachers in Brazil** and **55 % in Chile** used AI in the prior
year, against an **OECD average of 36 %**. **PISA 2025**: roughly **nine in ten 15-year-olds**
across the region's thirteen assessed countries have used AI chatbots for schoolwork. 🔴 Against
that, the **UNESCO IESALC study (September 2026)** finds only **26 % of higher-education
institutions have a formal AI strategy**, with adoption driven by faculty and students rather than
institutional policy. **UNESCO launched its regional Observatory on AI in Education for Latin
America and the Caribbean in April 2026, in Santiago, Chile.** The **IDB**'s review of **193
regional AI initiatives** found few with rigorous evaluation or robust safeguards. 🔴 **No national
AI-in-education statute surfaced for any LATAM country this pass** — searched, not found. The
regional instrument is UNESCO's Observatory, which is soft governance.

### 🟢 Key players and institutions, by region

| region | institutions that set the terms | commercial / platform presence |
|---|---|---|
| North America | state education agencies (North Carolina's K-12 AI guidance is the most detailed), NEA and AFT, the student-authored **"STUDENTS FIRST Act of 2026"** framework from all 50 states (July 2026) | White House AI-education executive order; a *Pledge to America's Youth* with **200+ tech companies** including Google, OpenAI, Microsoft, NVIDIA, Salesforce |
| EMEA | European Commission **Apply AI Alliance**; national authorities under the AI Act; EU AI Office | GPAI code-of-practice signatories reportedly include Amazon, Anthropic, Google, IBM, Microsoft, OpenAI, Mistral AI, Aleph Alpha, Cohere, ServiceNow; **xAI signed only the safety chapter; Meta declined** |
| APAC | Korea's AI Basic Act regime; Vietnam's high-risk decision; Japan and Singapore's voluntary frameworks; China's registration regime | market reports name Google, Microsoft, IBM, Pearson, Byju's; **UAE** cited for compulsory AI literacy. 🟡 These player lists are market-research sourced and unverified here |
| LATAM | **UNESCO** (regional Observatory, Santiago, April 2026), **UNESCO IESALC** + UNU-IAS, **IDB**, **Digital Education Council** (2026 LATAM survey, 30 000+ responses across 29 institutions), INCAE | 🔴 **no vendor share data found.** Named initiative: Brazil's **Vínculo**, an AI platform for students with disabilities, from the IDB's 193-solution review |

## Opportunities by region — 🔴 SUPERSEDED by `p473` (consolidated into the single current block above; content retained here and in `git log`)

### North America

🟢 **Compliance-shaped build, not capability-shaped.** 134 bills across 31 states means the binding
constraint is **provable** human oversight and student-data handling, not model quality. The
sellable artifact is an **audit trail**: every AI-touched decision about a student recorded with
the human who made it, exportable per state statute. Oklahoma and Maryland already require this
posture; California AB 1159 forbids training on student data, which makes **no-train, local, or
contractually-fenced inference** a procurement requirement rather than a preference.
🟢 **Build-on supply:** `OpenOLAT` (Apache-2.0) for a fork-and-own core, `vijju9019/GradeAI`'s
teacher-override cockpit as the human-in-the-loop reference, Moodle at the LTI boundary for the
installed base. 🔴 **Weakness:** the region with ≈36 % of the market has the **thinnest open-source
education supply on these shelves** — one granted North-American row (`grading_assistant`, 4★,
last pushed 2025-08). US edtech is commercial, so expect to integrate rather than compose.
🟢 **Timing:** H.R. 8747 would make federal funds spendable on AI literacy; a literacy-and-training
offer is the near-term entry, the governed assessment platform the follow-on.

### EMEA

🟢 **Sixteen months of runway, if the December-2027 reading holds** (`Gap 469-E`). High-risk
education obligations at **2 December 2027** means a system designed for Annex III conformity now
ships well inside the window — and the **AI literacy duty is already live (2 February 2025)**, so
staff-training work is sellable **today**, with no date to wait for. Two-thirds of European
education systems already have a strategy document, which is a buying signal: the strategy exists
and the implementation does not.
🟢 **Build-on supply:** `OpenOLAT` is Swiss-origin, Apache-2.0 and already carries QTI, SCORM and
assessment — the shortest path to an Annex-III-shaped assessment system. `insolitos/IA_para_aprender`
(MIT, Portugal) is a school-library AI-literacy app and a ready reference for the literacy duty.
🟢 **Sovereignty is a requirement, not a preference** — districts under data-residency rules are
already self-hosting open-weight models, which is exactly the Ollama/vLLM + local-RAG shape these
shelves carry.
🔴 **Gap:** no EMEA-specific market-size or vendor-share figure survived scrutiny this pass. Scope
EMEA deals on the regulatory calendar, not on a market number.

### APAC

🟢 **Jurisdiction-by-jurisdiction architecture is the product.** Korea's Act is in force (22 January
2026), Vietnam's bites 1 March 2027, Japan and Singapore are voluntary, China requires
registration, Australia is undecided. A multi-country APAC client cannot run one configuration, so
the sellable artifact is a **policy layer** — per-jurisdiction rules at the decision points, with
the same model stack underneath.
🟢 **Vietnam's carve-out is a design spec handed over for free:** high-risk applies **only where AI
output is the sole basis for a decision without meaningful human review**. Build meaningful human
review into assessment and the system is out of the high-risk tier by construction. The six-month
grace for systems live before **15 August 2026** rewards shipping early.
🟢 **Build-on supply is the strongest of any region here** — `DeepTutor` (Apache-2.0, 43 930★, HKU)
and `OpenMAIC` (MIT, 41 563★, Tsinghua) are both education-native, permissive and compounding.
🔴 **Gap, named:** that supply is **China and Hong Kong only**. One dedicated leg returned **no**
India- or Japan-originated open-source education agent. For engagements in India, Japan, Korea or
SEA these shelves offer composable supply but **no local project**, and an engagement there starts
from English/Chinese-language assets.

### LATAM

🟢 **The adoption-governance gap IS the engagement.** Teachers are at 56 % (Brazil) and 55 %
(Chile) against an OECD 36 %; students are at nine in ten; and only **26 % of higher-education
institutions have a formal AI strategy**. Faculty and students have already adopted what the
institution has not governed. The first deliverable is therefore **not** a tutor — it is the
institutional AI strategy, the acceptable-use policy and the assessment-integrity posture that the
existing usage already requires. The IDB's finding that few of 193 initiatives have rigorous
evaluation makes **measurement** the second deliverable, and it is a differentiator because almost
nobody has it.
🟢 **Build-on supply, now regionally placed:** `ThallesYVasconcelos/Agente-Educacional` (MIT) is
grounded in **BNCC, PCN and PNLD** with page-level citations and runs on local models via Ollama —
curriculum-aligned, Portuguese-language, and the single most reusable LATAM asset on these shelves.
`portabilis/i-educar` (GPL-2.0) is the installed SIS in Brazilian municipal secretariats.
`learningequality/kolibri` (MIT, offline-first) is the row for rural connectivity. `Chamilo 2.0`
(April 2026) has real regional footprint.
🟢 **Rubric-based assignment feedback — the opportunity this file has carried since `p464` — now
has granted supply** (four MIT rows, `agents/top.md`). 🔴 All four are 0–4★ single-maintainer
repositories, one last pushed in 2023. Price them as **starting points to fork and own**, not as
dependencies.
🔴 **Gaps:** no national AI-in-education statute found for any LATAM country; no vendor share data;
the regional instrument is UNESCO's Observatory (April 2026, Santiago), which is soft governance.
A LATAM engagement is therefore governed by institutional policy and procurement, not by statute —
which is why the strategy deliverable leads.

🔵 **Regions with no finding this pass, stated explicitly rather than left to look like coverage:**
Africa and the Middle East are **not** in this shelf's closed `region` vocabulary, so they have no
subsection by construction — the UAE's compulsory-AI-literacy mandate is filed under APAC above
because that is where the source placed it, and `p465`'s measurement of African education supply
(61 repositories, maximum 12★) is unchanged. No pass has yet produced a placed finding for either.

in that tier remains ungradeable** — and that is now a fourteen-month-old open question, not a
footnote. Any EMEA engagement quoting an AI Act date must quote both.

### 🟢 Education-specific high-risk classification — the cross-region pattern worth naming

🟢 Three jurisdictions now name **education** as a high-risk or specially regulated use of AI
rather than leaving it to general data-protection law: the **EU** (admissions and student
evaluation, Annex III), **Vietnam** (automated assessment and behavioural monitoring), and
**US states** individually (Oklahoma and Maryland barring AI from high-stakes student decisions;
NYC's traffic-light guidance barring AI from grading, discipline and IEP development).

🔵 For Globant the convergence matters more than any single rule: **automated assessment is the
regulated surface in every one of them.** A grading or placement feature is the part of an
education engagement that attracts an obligation, in three regions, under four different legal
instruments.

## Opportunities by region — 🔴 SUPERSEDED by `p473` (consolidated into the single current block above; content retained here and in `git log`)

### North America

🟢 **Policy-to-control translation.** 35+ state education departments have published AI guidance
and districts must adopt policies against dated deadlines (Ohio, 2026-07-01). The gap is between
a published policy and an enforceable control in the LMS. 🟢 Buildable on permissive supply:
[`jcputney/scorm-again`](https://github.com/jcputney/scorm-again) (MIT, verified this pass) for
the interop layer, an LMS MCP connector from the carried tier for the system of record.
🔴 **Hard constraint:** NYC-style guidance bars AI from grading, discipline and IEP development,
so the sellable product is an **audit and oversight layer**, not an autonomous grader.

🔴 **Declared supply gap:** zero novel North American build-on rows this pass.

### EMEA

🟡 **AI Act readiness for education providers, quoted with both deadline scenarios.** Enforcement
began 2026-08-02; the Digital Omnibus may move high-risk obligations to 2027-12 / 2028-08 and the
channel cannot resolve it. 🟢 The commercially honest offer is a **readiness assessment that
prices both timelines**, because a client told only the later date and then held to the earlier
one has been mis-advised.

🟢 **Self-hosted institutional delivery is the region's natural fit.** National funding is
directed at public systems (Denmark's €200M AI strategy including education; France's AI for
Humanity; the Netherlands' national AI strategy), and the platform supply is overwhelmingly
AGPL — which suits a self-hosted ministry buyer and is wrong for multi-tenant SaaS.
[`ai-swarm-solutions-group/OpenTeacherAgent`](https://gitlab.com/ai-swarm-solutions-group/OpenTeacherAgent)
(AGPL-3.0, verified this pass) is the new row here; `Selleo/mentingo` (MIT, verified) is the
permissive alternative when the buyer needs SaaS.

🔴 **Declared supply gap:** zero novel EMEA build-on rows this pass. Every EMEA address the legs
returned — Finland (`sisu-mcp`), Bosnia (`ibu-mcp`), Germany (`arsnova-server`), Poland
(`mentingo`) — was already carried.

### APAC

🟢 **Assessment compliance tooling, with Vietnam as the forcing function.** Vietnam's AI Law names
automated assessment and behavioural monitoring as high-risk; South Korea's AI Basic Act has been
in force since 2026-01-22. 🟢 The regional buyer most likely to pay is a **university or national
system that has already deployed automated assessment and now needs it documented.**

🟡 **Public opinion is the most favourable of any region.** Ipsos Education Monitor 2026: support
for *banning* AI in schools runs 23 % (Indonesia) to 31 % (South Korea), with Australia and New
Zealand the exception. 🔵 That is permission to deploy, not evidence of outcomes.

🔴 **The richest APAC curriculum data cannot be sold.** `xiaoyang111222/cn-math-taxonomy`
(4 029★, 1 644 topics, 2 403 prerequisite edges) is `CC-BY-NC` — verified again this pass.
Any China-adjacent curriculum product must source its graph elsewhere.

🔴 **Declared supply gap:** APAC's only novel address this pass,
[`mdakram2001/AMU-Utilities`](https://github.com/mdakram2001/AMU-Utilities) (India), serves **no
grant at ten root paths**.

### LATAM

🟢 **Institutional AI governance — and this is the region's clearest opportunity on any pass.**
UNESCO IESALC / UNU-IAS, 200 institutions across 19 countries: **87 % use AI in at least one
area; 26 % have a formal AI strategy.** Adoption is driven by individual academics and students
rather than institutions. 🟢 The product that follows is not a tutor: it is **institutional AI
governance as a delivered system** — an inventory of where AI is already in use, a policy
baseline, and the controls that make the policy checkable. The 61-point gap is the addressable
market, and it is already warm because the adoption happened first.

🟢 **The regulatory ground is firming, unevenly.** Uruguay is the first LATAM country to sign the
Council of Europe AI Framework Convention; Colombia adopted CONPES 4144; Peru's law is in force;
Mexico's federal bill sits in the Senate. UNESCO's LAC Observatory (2026-04-14) and the
Tec de Monterrey–UNESCO agreement give an engagement credible local anchors.

🔴 **Declared supply gap, and it is the sharpest one on this file.** The Spanish and Portuguese
discovery legs were the most productive of the pass by novel addresses (12 of 15) and returned
**zero** build-on rows. [`leonine1983/aprendix`](https://github.com/leonine1983/aprendix) — a
Brazilian school-management system with AI dropout support, the exact regional product shape —
serves **no grant at ten root paths**.
[`fabianastudillo/cafe-con-prompts`](https://github.com/fabianastudillo/cafe-con-prompts)
(Ecuador) is usable **as content under attribution**, not as software. 🔵 The operational
consequence: **a LATAM engagement is a build, not an assembly**, and should be priced that way.

### Global

🟢 **Study tooling is migrating from applications to agent skills.**
[`virgiliojr94/book-to-skill`](https://github.com/virgiliojr94/book-to-skill) is at **36 192★** —
the largest figure these shelves have ever carried — and it is a converter, not a product.

🔴 **Counter-signal, stated because it is also the market.**
[`rudra496/StealthHumanizer`](https://github.com/rudra496/StealthHumanizer) (189★, MIT, verified
this pass) exists to defeat Turnitin and GPTZero. Detection-evasion is a funded, permissively
licensed, actively maintained segment of education AI, and any assessment-integrity product
Globant sells is competing against it.

🔵 **Four legs, four nils. That is the finding, and it is not a failure of the legs.** It is the
same saturation these shelves have measured seven times on the supply side, now reproduced on the
**intelligence** side: a 466-pass base has absorbed the open macro channel for this industry. 🟢
The implication for the next pass is the same as for supply — **change the axis, not the effort**:
a country leg beats a region leg (`Gap 445-F` already prescribed this for EMEA), and a
primary-source leg (EUR-Lex, a ministry gazette, a national statistics office) beats a search leg.

### 🔴 `Gap 466-F`'s retirement is vindicated by the channel itself

🔵 `p455` retired *"134 bills / 31 states"* because three other trackers disagree by up to **2.6×**,
and `Gap 466-F` generalised the rule: **no forecast or count is publishable here without its basis
recorded, so the next pass can recompute it.** 🔴 This pass's North America leg returned that very
figure as its lead statistic, from a tracker blog, with no methodology. 🟢 **A retired number that
keeps coming back is not a settled number — it is a popular one.** The cell stays retired; the
honest statement remains that US AI-in-education legislation is **state-level, numerous and
counted differently by every tracker**, and any specific count must name its tracker and its
inclusion rule.

🟡 **The one new figure that survives the `466-F` test.** K12 Academics' ≈ **$6B (2024) → ≈ $32B
(2030)** implies a **32.2 %** CAGR, which is internally consistent and sits *inside* the
`$6.4B`–`$11.4B` 2026 range this base already publishes across four firms. 🟢 It is therefore
carried as a **corroborating low-end anchor**, with its base year, horizon and implied CAGR
recorded as `466-F` requires — and explicitly **not** as a replacement for the range.

## Opportunities by region — 🔴 SUPERSEDED by `p473` (consolidated into the single current block above; content retained here and in `git log`)

### North America

🟡 **Shape: compliance-grade human review, sold as a feature.** Oklahoma and Maryland have moved to
require **human oversight of high-stakes decisions about students**, and the AASA student-authored
framework asks for **appeal rights and human review of AI decisions**. 🟢 The build-on supply for
this is on these shelves and permissive: `tomdxb0004/eu-ai-act-risk-checker` (MIT) for obligation
triage, `ucfopen/UDOIT` (GPL-3.0-or-later) for accessibility conformance inside the LMS, and
`scorm-again` + `tsugi` (MIT / Apache-2.0) as the carriers into an existing estate. 🔴 **The
regulatory count is contested** (see `466-F`), so scope the engagement to the *mechanism* —
reviewable, appealable, logged decisions — not to a bill number.

### EMEA

🟢 **Shape: sovereignty-shaped delivery, and the licence is a feature.** Annex III education
obligations land **2027-12-02** and Art. 50 transparency is already live; Art. 5(1)(f)'s
**emotion-inference prohibition** has been in force since 2025-02-02 with no consent exception.
🟢 Two first-hand supply facts from this pass make the pitch concrete: GitLab-sourced EMEA
platforms are **AGPL-3.0** — which *fits* a ministry or public university that self-hosts — and
there is now **one permissive EMEA platform** read first-hand,
[`Selleo/mentingo`](https://github.com/Selleo/mentingo) (MIT, Polish), for the cases where the
client needs to resell. 🔵 The FRIA duty for public bodies is the billable artefact.

### APAC

🟢 **Shape: compose the agents, license or build the content.** The permissive **agent** tier is
genuinely strong — 12 of 22 placed APAC rows are `PERMISSIVE`, led by `DeepTutor` (Apache-2.0,
43 794★) and a dense exam-prep/tutoring skill layer. 🔴 **The content tier is commercially closed:**
all three NonCommercial rows in the population are Chinese-language, including the window's richest
curriculum graph (`cn-math-taxonomy`, `CC-BY-NC`, 1 644 knowledge points / 2 403 prerequisite
edges). 🔵 Regulatory shape is **per-jurisdiction and binding in three of them** (Korea's AI Basic
Act, China's registration regime, Vietnam's education-naming high-risk decree with its
2026-08-15 grace cutoff), so a multi-country APAC programme carries **policy as a per-market
configuration**, not a global setting. 🔴 Declared gap: `gitee.com` is DNS-unreachable from this
environment, so every APAC supply figure here is a **floor**.

### LATAM

🔴 **Shape: build and localise — do not expect to source locally.** The demand signal is the
strongest on this file (UNESCO IESALC **87 %** adoption against **26 %** with a formal strategy;
92 %-student / 19 %-faculty on assignment feedback; ~30 % of universities with a published AI
policy) and the local open-source supply is **1 row in 103, archived**. 🟢 So the engagement is a
**build on globally-sourced permissive components with Portuguese/Spanish localisation and a
governance deliverable** — the governance half being exactly the 26 %-with-a-strategy gap. 🔵
`menthorlabs/menthor` (Apache-2.0) remains usable as a *reference* for Portuguese-language
pedagogy structure; it is archived and not a dependency.

### Global

🟢 **Shape: the grant-class audit itself is billable.** This pass measured that **sellability is
flat across every star band** (0.53–0.68) while grant presence rises with stars — so an
institution's own "we'll use the popular open-source one" screen has **no information** about
whether the thing can be delivered. 🔴 Of 103 visible education repositories, **5 are
`UNSELLABLE`**, **3 are content-not-software**, **2 grant per-asset**, **1 points at another
repository** and **11 have no grant at all** — i.e. **22 of 103 (21 %)** cannot be used as a client
screen would assume. A short licence-clearance engagement ahead of any platform build pays for
itself on the first `*-NC` corpus it catches.
