## 🆕 `p466` — **🔴 THIS BASE'S OWN HEADLINE FORECAST FAILS AN ARITHMETIC CHECK AGAINST ITS SOURCE'S LONGER HORIZON: the carried `$9.58B → $42.48B (2030)` implies a 45.1 % CAGR, while the same firm's 2035 forecast from the SAME 2026 base implies 34.5 % — and 34.52 % compounded to 2030 gives `$31.4B`, not `$42.48B`. 🟢 Two regulatory deadlines land within FIVE WEEKS of this pass: EU Art. 50 content-marking (2026-12-02) and Chile's Ley 21.719 (2026-12-01). 🟢 `Gap 465-D` is CLOSED — the Kenya leg returns four named instruments.**

### 🔴 The arithmetic defect in the carried forecast

🔵 `Gap 464-A` registered that no gate here grades a **prose claim** against another shelf. This is
the same class, one rung harder: a claim that is internally inconsistent with **its own source's**
other published horizon.

| figure | source horizon | implied CAGR from the common `$9.58B` 2026 base |
|---|---|---|
| 🔵 carried on this shelf: **`$42.48B` by 2030** | 4 years | **45.1 %** |
| 🟢 read this pass: **`$136.79B` by 2035**, firm-stated CAGR **34.52 %** | 9 years | **34.4 %** — 🟢 reproduces the firm's own stated figure |
| 🔴 **34.52 % compounded from `$9.58B` to 2030** | 4 years | **`$31.4B`** |

🔴 **So the carried 2030 figure sits 35 % above what the same firm's own CAGR produces**, and both
numbers have been published here as if on one curve. 🟢 **Correction applied in place:** the 2026
base (`$9.58B`) is reproduced and retained; the long horizon is restated as **`$136.79B` by 2035 at
34.52 %** (Precedence Research); the `$42.48B`-by-2030 cell is **retired** as not reconcilable with
it. 🔵 Independent cross-check from a second firm, carried as a range not a point: **`$11.4B`
(2026) → `$57.2B` (2033) at 25.9 %**, and a round-up of four firms spanning **`$6.4B`–`$11.4B`**
for 2026. 🔴 **The honest cell is the range, not any point.** Registered as **`Gap 466-F`**.

### 🔵 Regional share, as measured rather than asserted

| region | share / growth | basis |
|---|---|---|
| North America | **36 %** of the global AI-in-education market — largest single share | market-research aggregate |
| APAC | 🟢 **fastest-growing, 35.3 % CAGR** | same aggregate |
| EMEA · LATAM · MEA | 🔴 **no share figure returned on any leg this pass** | an informed gap, recorded per the ingest brief rather than left silent |

## Opportunities by region — 🔴 SUPERSEDED by `p473` (consolidated into the single current block above; content retained here and in `git log`)

### North America

🟢 **The buyable work is policy-to-practice conversion, and the volume is now statutory.** More than
**1 500** AI-related bills were introduced across the states this year, with roughly **100**
directly touching K-12 student AI use, and ~**34 states + DC + Puerto Rico** have issued guidance.
Federal curriculum standards remain **absent** — the `K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act`
(**H.R. 8747**) has only advanced in committee, and would unlock *existing* federal funding streams
for AI-literacy procurement rather than mandate content.

Named instruments that create dated obligations:

| instrument | obligation | status read this pass |
|---|---|---|
| **Idaho SB 1227** | statewide K-12 generative-AI framework: local policies, AI-literacy standards, data-privacy rules, and a **bar on AI replacing teachers** | 🟢 signed **2026-03-19**, effective **2026-07-01** |
| **Ohio Rev. Code 3301.24** | **every district** must adopt a board-approved AI policy | 🟢 carried, in force |
| **Oklahoma SB 1734** | age-appropriate tools, **teacher review of AI output**, **parent opt-out**, district policies | 🟢 before the **2027-28** school year |
| **Maryland SB 720** | SDE to publish AI guidance, run professional development, help districts evaluate tools | 🔴 **status contested across three trackers** (effective 2026-06-01 / signed 2026-05-26 / passed one chamber). Relayed, **not** relied on |
| **Arizona HB 4040** | K-12 **and public universities** to adopt student AI-use policies | 🟡 **introduced, not enacted** |

🔵 **The offer that fits:** a district-level *AI policy conformance* engagement — inventory tools,
map each to the state's duty (teacher-review, opt-out, literacy standard), and instrument the
evidence. 🟢 Oklahoma's **teacher review of AI output** and Idaho's **no-replacement** clause are
both requirements that `Pattern BJ` on [`compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md) satisfies
architecturally — a human-arbitrated, deterministic-scoring assessment spine — rather than by
policy text.

### EMEA

🔴 **A deadline inside five weeks, and it is the one nobody is briefing.** Art. **50**
transparency duties (chatbot disclosure, synthetic-content marking) apply from **2026-08-02**, and
systems **already on the market** have until **2026-12-02** to add content marking. 🟢 That is
**~5 weeks from this pass** and it bites every AI feature already live in an EU institution.

| instrument | what it does | date |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 **Art. 5(1)(f)** | **prohibits** inferring emotions in educational institutions (medical/safety exception), to **€35 M / 7 %** | 🔴 **in force since 2025-02-02** — a live prohibition, not runway |
| 🔴 **Art. 50** | chatbot disclosure + synthetic-content marking; **existing** systems must comply | 🔴 **2026-12-02** |
| 🟢 **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** | **moves** stand-alone high-risk (Annex III) education systems from **2026-08-02** to **2027-12-02** | 🟢 amendment read this pass |
| 🟡 Commission draft guidelines (May 2026) | **tutoring, practice feedback and information chatbots generally fall OUTSIDE Annex III**; admissions, learning-steering grading and proctoring are inside | 🟡 draft |
| 🟡 FRIA | public universities (and private bodies providing a public service) need a fundamental-rights impact assessment before first use | 🟡 with the high-risk regime |

🔵 **The offer that fits, and it now has matching supply.** The Annex III slip to **2027-12-02**
buys institutions 14 months — so the sellable work in the window is **Art. 50 marking + Art. 5
prohibition audit**, then sovereign-stack readiness. 🟢 This pass found the EMEA supply for the
second half on a forge this base had never sampled: `DD-workspace/DD` (Xnet, Barcelona),
`moodlenet` (federated OER) and `Openki` — all **AGPL-3.0**, which fits institutional self-hosting
and **not** multi-tenant SaaS. See [`repos/trending.md`](../repos/trending.md) and `Pattern BI`.

### APAC

🟢 **The fastest-growing region (35.3 % CAGR) and the only one where the state is the system
integrator**, which changes who the buyer is.

| market | named instrument | what it commits to |
|---|---|---|
| **China** | 🟢 MOE **"AI Plus Education" action plan** (2026-04-10) + the **2026-03-31** priorities meeting | a comprehensive AI education system **primary school → university, full implementation by 2030**, with pilots and 🔵 explicitly **strengthened data-security and personal-information protection** as AI spreads in education |
| **Singapore** | 🟢 MOE **Authoring Copilot** (teacher lesson/assessment material) + **Learning Assistant** platform; four principles **Agency · Inclusivity · Fairness · Safety** | 🟢 a **phased age gate**: lower primary kept away from AI tools, upper primary introduced **under close teacher supervision**. Minister Desmond Lee confirmed MOE is formally studying AI's impact (**2026-02-25**) |
| **India** | 🟡 AI + computational thinking **mandatory from Class 3**, 2026-27 | 🔴 **single secondary source** — relayed, not relied on |
| **South Korea** | 🟡 **AI Basic Act** | 🔴 sources conflict: "in force January 2026" vs "due during 2026" |

🔵 Adoption asymmetry worth pricing: working-age generative-AI use **Singapore 64.3 % vs Japan
24.7 %** (June 2026). 🟢 **The offer that fits:** teacher-workload copilots procured to a
*published* national framework. Singapore's four principles and China's data-protection emphasis are
both answerable with the on-device/no-egress design in `Pattern BJ`; 🟢 APAC is also where the
**skill-packaged** supply is densest (`kaogong-skill`, `flysheep-ai/education-skills`, zh-CN), so the
localisation asset already exists.

### LATAM

🔴 **A deadline inside five weeks here too, and it is the harder one.** **Chile's Ley 21.719**
(data protection) enters into force **2026-12-01** and already regulates **automated profiling and
algorithmic decision-making** — which is what adaptive learning and automated grading *are*. 🟢 It
binds before any Chilean AI statute does; the risk-based AI bill is still in `trámite`.

| market | instrument | status |
|---|---|---|
| **Chile** | 🔴 **Ley 21.719** — automated profiling + algorithmic decisions | 🔴 **in force 2026-12-01** |
| **Brazil** | **PL 2338/2023** | 🟡 Senate-approved Dec 2024, **still pending in the Câmara dos Deputados** |
| **Mexico** | "Ley Nahui" initiative | 🟡 pending |

🟢 **The governance gap is measured, and it is the opportunity.** UNESCO: in **Chile and Brazil
more than 50 % of teachers already use these tools**, while **fewer than 10 % of institutions in
the region have formal guidelines and sufficient capacity**. 🔵 **So the deficit is institutional
governance, not adoption** — the inverse of the North American picture, where policy is racing
ahead of practice. UNESCO has stood up an **Observatory of AI in Education for Latin America and
the Caribbean**, and the **ILIA 2026** (CEPAL + CENIA) index reads **Chile 71.4 · Brazil 68.4 ·
Uruguay 67.0 · Mexico 52.0**, with **11** of the analysed countries now carrying AI content in
school curricula against **2 in 2024**. Demand-side anchor: **Tec de Monterrey** is redesigning
**44+** degree programmes for full AI integration.

🔴 **And the supply-side warning reproduces.** For the **second consecutive pass**, the repository
matching this region's clearest opportunity — rubric-based assignment feedback — is **ungranted**:
`p464`'s row, and this pass's Brazilian `henriquegpb/gradesinteli` (86★, `NO-ROOT-GRANT`). 🟢 The
one granted LATAM row found is `menthorlabs/menthor` (**Apache-2.0**, Portuguese, teaching
platform) — 🔴 a platform, **not** feedback. **The LATAM feedback gap cannot be closed with
carried supply**; it must be built. That is `Pattern BK`.

### Global

🟢 **Market frame, as a range:** **`$6.4B`–`$11.4B`** in 2026 across four firms; the longest
defensible horizon read first-hand is **`$136.79B` by 2035 at 34.52 %** from a `$9.58B` 2026 base.
🔴 The `$42.48B`-by-2030 cell this shelf carried is retired as arithmetically irreconcilable with
it (above).

🟢 **The cross-regional structural finding of this pass:** the **forge you source from selects your
delivery model**. GitHub education supply read this pass is MIT/Apache-dominant (9 of 12 granted
rows permissive) and suits multi-tenant SaaS; GitLab education supply is **AGPL-3.0 on 4 of 5
granted rows** and suits institutional self-hosting — which is precisely the EMEA sovereignty and
LATAM public-sector buyer. 🔵 A studio sourcing only from GitHub systematically under-serves both.

🔴 **And the licence trap is global, not regional:** of 12 granted GitHub rows read first-hand this
pass, **three are unsellable or restricted** — `CC-BY-NC-4.0` (a 4 000★ maths knowledge graph),
`Elastic-2.0` (a 340★ higher-ed advising platform) and `CC-BY-SA-4.0` (the 914★ teacher-skills
library). See `Gap 466-A` on [`agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md).

no open supply to meet it**. Every other region on this file has some open layer to compose from;

has never stated it.

## Opportunities by region — 🔴 SUPERSEDED by `p466` (consolidated into the single current block above; content retained here and in `git log`)

### North America
🔵 **State guidance is now the norm and the binding edge has moved to procurement and data.**
Roughly **33–35 states plus Puerto Rico** have issued AI-in-education guidance (up from ~28 in
early 2025) — 🔴 **carried as a range, not a number**: trackers disagree, and `p455` retired a
single-tracker figure on exactly this axis. **Ohio** and **Tennessee** legally require districts to
adopt AI policies (Ohio's deadline **2026-07-01**, already passed); **Oklahoma**'s applies before
the 2027-28 school year. 🟢 **Where the work is:** Alabama's procurement model policy recommends a
**data-usage-restriction clause** in district contracts and California's **A.B. 1159** (proposed)
would bar training models on student data — so the sellable artefact is a **contract-and-data-flow
control layer**, not a tutor. 🟢 `oas114/EduShield` (MIT, client-side de-identification, on
[`agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md)) is the first permissive component this base carries that maps
directly onto that clause, despite being built for Taiwan's PDPA.

### EMEA
🔵 **A deferred high-risk regime over a live prohibition — and now a third tier underneath both.**
`Regulation (EU) 2026/1744` (Digital Omnibus) moved stand-alone high-risk education AI to
**2027-12-02**; Art. 50 chatbot transparency has been live since **2026-08-02**; the Art. 4
AI-literacy duty since **2025-02-02**; the Art. 5(1)(f) **emotion-recognition prohibition in
education is in force now**. 🔴 All carried since `p380` — this hour's EMEA leg was **confirmation,
not discovery**. 🟢 **What is new is beneath the EU**: Nigeria's **NUC** pressed universities on
2026-10-13 to accelerate AI adoption *with* tighter compliance, and a **national university AI
policy** was announced (🔴 contents and timeline unconfirmed) — which would be the first, since a
2025 study found **no Ghanaian or Nigerian HEI** with a formal public AI policy. Burkina Faso's
`naabiga-os` and Ghana's `ghanaschools` registry place francophone and anglophone West African
supply on these shelves for the first time. 🔴 **Kenya returned nothing on intel** while returning
MIT-granted supply — `Gap 465-D`.

### APAC
🔵 **Mandates at national scale, and one instructive reversal.** China: AI compulsory from **age
6** since 2025-09, with provincial hour floors (Beijing **8 h/yr** primary and secondary; Guangdong
6 h in lower grades rising to a fortnightly hour in grades 10–11) over the 2024 MOE three-tier
framework. India: **AI + Computational Thinking mandatory from Class 3 in 2026-27** under NEP 2020
— 🔴 gated by **>10 million teachers** needing preparation, which is the single largest
training-services line item on this file. 🔴 **South Korea is the cautionary case**: mandatory AI
textbook adoption sat **below 30 %** by March and the National Assembly **stripped the textbooks of
official status** that August after unions said the rollout had outrun preparation. Japan moves
slowly — digital textbooks may become official only around the **2030** school year. 🟢 All
carried; the APAC-specific *supply* added this pass is `Maicarons/RubricSpan` (AGPL-3.0, Chinese
subjective-question marking) and `oas114/EduShield` (MIT, Taiwan).

### LATAM
🔵 **Adoption is ahead of the rules, and the rules are moving this quarter.** Brazil's **PL
2338/2023** passed the Senate (2024-12-10), sits in the Chamber of Deputies with the vote expected
to resume **after the October 2026 elections**, and its risk tiers put **education among high-risk
uses** requiring impact assessment and human oversight — the most consequential pending instrument
in the region for this industry. Chile's AI bill (bulletin 16821-19) is in committee, and the
government has asked to move Law **21.719**'s general effective date from 2026-12-01 to
**2027-12-01**. 🔴 **Mexico's status is genuinely contested** in this hour's sources — one legal
guide describes enacted 2026 amendments creating binding obligations, others say no AI bill has
passed — and it is carried as **conflicting**, not resolved. **Peru remains the only enacted AI law
in LATAM.** 🟡 Meanwhile classroom AI use is described as proceeding largely without oversight or
guidelines. 🔴 **The LATAM supply nil persists**: the rubric-feedback shape this file has named as
LATAM's clearest opportunity for six passes now has a permissive implementation
(`erlan775959-code/markwise`, MIT) but **no LATAM-placed one** — and `markwise` is at 0★.

### Global
🔵 **The cross-region constant is the governance-to-capability gap**, and it is what a studio sells
into. Directionally (🟡 **Futuresource Consulting**, one house, uncorroborated): K-12 and HE are
moving from experimentation to structured implementation, with AI-capable devices at ~**15 %** of
institutional PC shipments in 2026 toward half by 2030; agentic AI and **small, curriculum-specific
models** are flagged as emerging, partly driven by the EU AI Act. 🔴 **No market-size figure is
carried**: this hour's sources ranged from $6.4 B to >$100 B for overlapping definitions, and per
`p455`'s precedent a spread that wide is reported as a spread or not at all. 🟢 The durable,
first-hand global fact from this pass is a **supply** one: across every leg run, **the permissive
marking/feedback tier tops out at 3★ while the adopted tier is GPL/AGPL** — so assessment work is
a copyleft-boundary decision in every region.

### 🔴 The EMEA finding, stated plainly because it inverts how this file frames the region

🔵 This file's EMEA section says the deliverable is an **Annex III conformity posture** and that
**"13 months to 2027-12-02 is a build runway, not a scramble"**. 🟢 That remains true for
admissions, evaluation and exam scoring. 🔴 **But it is not the whole duty, and the part this file
omitted is already enforceable:**

| instrument | applies since | what it forbids |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 **Art. 5(1)(f)** — prohibited practice, **not** high-risk | 🔴 **2025-02-02 — in force for over 20 months** | AI that **infers emotions** in educational institutions. 🔴 Reported scope includes inferring **student attention or interest** from camera or voice, and emotion detection in admission tests. 🔵 **No consent exception**; narrow medical / safety exception only |
| 🟡 **Art. 27 FRIA** | with the high-risk regime | a **Fundamental Rights Impact Assessment** before a public body deploys a high-risk education system |

🔴 **The engagement consequence, and it is the opposite of a runway:** engagement-detection,
attention-scoring and "is the student focused?" telemetry — standard features in proctoring and
classroom-analytics products, and a common ask in an edtech brief — are **already prohibited in
the EU**, at up to €35 M or 7 % of global turnover. 🟢 A Globant EMEA engagement has **no runway
at all on that feature class**: the correct answer at proposal stage is to decline it and offer
behaviour-free alternatives (task completion, attempt counts, mastery signals from
`CAHLR/OATutor`-style Bayesian tracing), which this base can already compose.
🟡 Institution-level movers named by the same legs — **Utrecht University** (risk-classified AI
policy) and the **University of Edinburgh** (Act-aligned governance guidelines) — are relayed as
instances. 🔴 **`Gap 456-D` stays OPEN**: these are policies, not adoption *rates*.

### 🟡 The LATAM finding — a standing negative is NARROWED, not refuted, and the distinction is the point

🔵 This file states: **"No binding education-AI instrument in any LATAM jurisdiction surfaced on
any leg."** 🟢 This pass found one that is education-specific:

| element | reading |
|---|---|
| instrument | reform to **Art. 61 of the Ley de Educación del Estado de México** |
| what it does | state education authorities **shall promote**, among teachers and students at **upper-secondary and higher** level, the **responsible, ethical and gradual** use of AI for educational purposes; progressive provision of ICT access incl. free internet in educational spaces for academic use |
| 🔵 scope carve-out | **basic education is deliberately excluded**, on a stated cognitive-maturity rationale |
| origin | Dip. **Lilia Urbina Salazar** (PRI), filed **November 2025** |
| 🟢 what is confirmed | the **Congreso del Estado de México's own channel** states the united committees approved it **unanimously** |
| 🔴 what is **not** confirmed | plenary date (a legislative observatory says **2026-04-27/28**), **publication in the Gaceta del Gobierno**, and **entry into force** — none found on any leg |

🟢 **Therefore the claim is narrowed, not refuted.** It holds as stated for *national* instruments
and for *in-force* ones. It does **not** hold as "nothing education-specific exists in LATAM
law-making" — a **sub-national** education-AI statute was approved by a state legislature in
April 2026. 🔴 And even if in force it is **hortatory, not a risk regime**: it imposes a duty to
*promote* responsible use, with no classification, conformity or oversight obligation.
🟢 **Which makes it demand, not compliance** — a state that must promote responsible AI use across
upper-secondary and higher education needs teacher training, institutional policy and tooling, and
that is a procurement, not a burden. Registered as `Gap 464-E` (confirm the Gaceta publication).

🟢 **Brazil, added because its absence read as coverage.** LATAM's largest education market has
**no AI statute in force**, and the delay now has a date and a named cause: `PL 2338/2023` passed
the Senate on **2024-12-10**, reached the Câmara on **2025-03-17**, and has sat in special
committee awaiting the rapporteur's report, with the rapporteur stating on **2026-08-24** that the
vote waits for after the **October 2026** elections. 🔵 This is the mechanism behind this file's
own thesis: **in LATAM demand arrived before governance**, and in Brazil governance is now
explicitly parked behind an electoral calendar.

## Opportunities by region — 🔴 SUPERSEDED by `p465` (consolidated into the single current block above; content retained here and in `git log`)

### North America

🔵 **Regulatory shape: no federal floor, fifty state clocks** — unchanged, and the federal layer
moved a step this year.

| signal | detail |
|---|---|
| 🟡 Federal, in committee | **K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026 (H.R. 8747)** — advanced at a House Education and Workforce markup, **2026-07-21**. 🔴 Committee passage is not law; recorded as a clock, not a duty |
| 🟢 Student-data law | **California AB 1159** — bars using student data to train AI models |
| 🟢 Human-oversight law | **Oklahoma** and **Maryland** — require human oversight and bar AI from sole high-stakes decisions about students |
| 🔵 District-level practice | **Katy ISD** phases supervised access from grade 7; **Charleston County** bars AI as sole basis for discipline, placement or special-education determinations |
| 🟡 Non-governmental | **STUDENTS FIRST Act of 2026**, a student-written framework (Day of AI / AASA) — would bar AI-generated written and artistic assignments at every grade. Not law |
| 🔵 Canada | **"AI for All"** strategy, **2026-06-04** — national AI-literacy commitment, 🔴 no AI statute |
| 🔵 Mexico | bills stacked, 🔴 no regulator to enforce them |
| 🟢 **Ohio — a duty whose deadline has PASSED** (`p464`) | **Ohio Rev. Code 3301.24**, enacted in the **HB 96** budget: every school district, community (charter) school and STEM school **must have adopted an AI policy by 2026-07-01**. The statute requires a policy to *exist* and does not prescribe its contents; the state published an optional **model policy**. 🔵 The first NA instrument on this file whose compliance date is behind us — the question in an Ohio engagement is no longer *whether* a policy exists but whether it is implementable |
| 🟡 **Idaho — relayed, sources disagree** (`p464`) | **SB 1227**, reported signed **2026-03-19**, effective **2026-07-01**, establishing a statewide generative-AI framework for public education. 🔴 Three trackers describe it three different ways — AI-literacy standards / a district AI framework with a designated governance role / a student-data-privacy mandate aimed at AI edtech — and disagree on the district deadline (before the 2026-27 school year vs 120 days after state guidance). 🔴 **Not promoted; the statute text was not reached on any leg** |

🟢 **One hard survey figure, and it is the only adoption number this pass promotes anywhere:**
**60 %** of U.S. K-12 teachers used AI tools during the 2024-25 school year and **32 %** used them
at least weekly.

🟢 **Where the work is.** "Sole basis" and "human oversight" clauses are *architecture
requirements*, not policy text: they demand a recorded human decision point and a reason string
per determination. 🔴 **This pass recovered the component that argument needs and that this base
had silently dropped:** [`pytorch/opacus`](https://github.com/pytorch/opacus) (Apache-2.0,
10 241 B · `bcf6964d43d8`) for training that cannot memorise a student, beside the carried
`CAHLR/OATutor` (MIT) for auditable Bayesian knowledge tracing. Recipe: `Pattern AX`.

### EMEA

🟢 **`p461`'s date is REPRODUCED from an independent leg this hour**, which matters because the
two deadline stories circulating are a year apart:

| instrument | date | status |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **Digital Omnibus, Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** — OJ **2026-07-24** | stand-alone **Annex III** high-risk (education access & assessment) → **2027-12-02**; AI embedded in regulated products → **2028-08-02** | 🟢 **reproduced, two independent legs, two passes** |
| 🔴 the superseded reading | "high-risk applies from **2026-08**" | 🔴 **refused** — it is the pre-Omnibus date and still in circulation |
| 🟡 Commission timeline | Omnibus entry into force **2026-07-27** | 🟡 relayed; sources disagree with the OJ date by three days and nothing turns on it |
| 🔴 **Art. 5(1)(f) — PROHIBITED PRACTICE, in force since 2025-02-02** (`p464`) | AI that **infers emotions in educational institutions** is **banned now**, not in 2027: reported scope includes inferring **student attention or interest** from camera or voice and emotion detection in admission tests; **no consent exception**, narrow medical/safety exception only; penalties to **€35 M / 7 %** of global turnover | 🟢 **PROMOTED — and it was missing from this file.** 🔴 EMEA does **not** have 13 months of runway on this feature class; it has had a live prohibition for over 20 months |
| 🟡 **Art. 27 FRIA** (`p464`) | a **Fundamental Rights Impact Assessment** before a public body deploys a high-risk education system | 🟡 relayed — rides the Annex III clock to 2027-12-02 |

🔵 Education sits in **Annex III**: admissions, student evaluation and exam scoring are
**high-risk**, and the Act reaches non-EU systems whose outputs affect EU students.
Non-binding context: UNESCO's generative-AI guidance for education; UK **principles-based** (AI
Opportunities Action Plan, Jan 2025); Germany's **€20 bn** AI commitment 2025-2030.

🔴 **`Gap 456-D` stays OPEN** — still no sourceable EMEA *institution-level* adoption rate; the
nearest offers remain workforce figures (~30 % of EU teachers trained, ~19.95 % EU enterprise
adoption), which are not it.

🔴 **A COVERAGE GAP THIS PASS DECLARES RATHER THAN HIDES: the Middle East and Africa returned
NOTHING.** Every EMEA leg resolved to EU-27 plus the UK and Germany. MEA is **one third of the
region's name and zero of its evidence here** — and that is a statement about this base's
sampling, not about MEA. 🟢 The one thing this pass can offer against it is supply, not
intelligence: [`kevindegila/construire-un-llm-de-zero`](https://github.com/kevindegila/construire-un-llm-de-zero)
(Apache-2.0) is a **francophone** curriculum, and francophone Africa is the largest education
market this base has never sampled. Registered as `Gap 463-D`.

🟢 **Where the work is.** **13 months** to 2027-12-02 is a build runway, not a scramble, and the
deliverable is an Annex III conformity posture: `pytorch/opacus` for the privacy control, an
Open edX **XBlock** (Apache-2.0) as the seam over an AGPL platform, and a recorded human decision
point at every scoring step. Recipe: `Pattern AX`.

system that can show what an answer rested on — which is exactly

### LATAM

🟢 **Adoption is the highest this base has recorded anywhere; governance is the lowest. Both
numbers come from the same hour's legs.**

| signal | figure | source posture |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 Student engagement | **92 %** | Digital Education Council LATAM survey 2026 — **30 000+** responses, **29** institutions. 🟡 Self-run survey, self-selected respondents: relayed with its method stated |
| 🟢 Faculty engagement | **79 %** | same |
| 🟢 Faculty sentiment | **72 %** positive vs **57 %** globally | same — 🔵 LATAM faculty are *more* AI-positive than their global peers |
| 🔴 The gap that is the opportunity | only **19 %** of faculty use AI for assignment feedback, though **half of students support it** | same |
| 🔴 Integrity anxiety | **61 %** of students fear peer misuse | same |

🔴 **No binding *national* education-AI instrument in any LATAM jurisdiction surfaced on any leg**
— 🔵 **narrowed by `p464`, which found a sub-national one.** The **Estado de México** reform to
**Art. 61 of its state Ley de Educación** is education-specific and was approved **unanimously in
united committees** (confirmed by the Congreso del Estado de México's own channel), with a
legislative observatory recording plenary approval on **2026-04-27/28**; 🔴 **Gaceta del Gobierno
publication and entry into force were not reached on any leg** (`Gap 464-E`), and the text is
**hortatory** — a duty to *promote* responsible, ethical and gradual AI use at upper-secondary and
higher level, with **basic education deliberately excluded** on a cognitive-maturity rationale —
so it carries **no classification, conformity or oversight obligation**.
🟢 **Brazil, so that silence does not read as coverage:** the region's largest education market has
**no AI statute in force**. `PL 2338/2023` passed the Senate **2024-12-10**, reached the Câmara
**2025-03-17**, and sits in special committee awaiting the rapporteur's report; rapporteur
**Aguinaldo Ribeiro** stated on **2026-08-24** that the vote waits until after the **October 2026**
elections.
🟢 Stated as a finding, now with a mechanism: the region's constraint is **not** regulatory
latency in the sense of drafting — it is that **demand has arrived before governance**, and in
Brazil governance is explicitly parked behind an electoral calendar. That is the opposite of
EMEA's problem.

🟢 **Where the work is, and it follows directly from the 92 %/19 % split.** Students have adopted;
faculty have not adopted the *feedback* workflow they would most benefit from, and integrity
anxiety is the stated blocker. The sellable engagement is **assessment-feedback with an audit
trail** — not a tutor. 🔴 **And it must be built on the integration tier, because this pass
measured the student-facing OSS tier at 6-of-6 ungranted** (see the LATAM gap above):
`mcp-brasil/mcp-brasil`, `bmosoluciones/now-lms` and `p443`'s `siu-guarani-mcp` behind an MCP
boundary. Recipe: `Pattern AZ`.

### Global

🔵 **The cross-region structure this pass can state, which no single region shows:**

1. 🔴 **The compliance calendar SPLIT by region in 2026.** EMEA postponed its education high-risk
   duty to **2027-12-02** while Vietnam, Korea, China and Taiwan brought general or
   education-specific duties **into force**. A multi-region programme can no longer carry one
   compliance posture: **an engagement in Hanoi has a live duty, one in Frankfurt has 13 months.**
   That divergence is the single most engagement-relevant fact on this file.
2. 🟢 **Evidence, not capability, is the binding constraint everywhere.** Buyers in every region
   now ask for proof of learning gain, and 🔴 **the open-source supply still has almost nothing to
   offer them**: of this pass's 12 novel rows, exactly **one**
   ([`ai4bharat/indicifeval`](https://github.com/ai4bharat/indicifeval), recovered rather than
   discovered) is an evaluation harness. The efficacy-evaluation gap stands.
3. 🟢 **The platform layer is copyleft almost end to end, but NOT end to end — corrected by
   `p464`.** 🔴 The `p462` form of this item said *"of ten carried platforms exactly one —
   **Kolibri**, MIT — is freely redistributable"*, and **this base's own `agents/top.md`
   contradicts it**: [`OpenOLAT/OpenOLAT`](https://github.com/OpenOLAT/OpenOLAT) is carried at
   **Apache-2.0** (payload re-read this pass: 10 982 B · `6dbc5b605ed0` @ HEAD `891b9434e822`) in
   a cell that calls it *"the only Apache-2.0 one… strongly under-used"*. 🟢 **At least two
   platform rows are freely redistributable — Kolibri (MIT) and OpenOLAT (Apache-2.0,
   university-grade LMS)** — and **Sakai** is unsettled on this base (ECL-2.0 in two places,
   Apache-2.0 in a third). 🔵 **Mechanism:** the count was computed from the eight-platform
   census on `verticals/solutions.md` and stated about *all* carried platforms, while OpenOLAT is
   shelved on `agents/top.md` (`Gap 464-A`). 🟢 Build on extension seams and keep proprietary work
   across the process boundary **by default** — but an engagement that must *ship* an LMS inside a
   redistributable deliverable **does** have a permissive option.
4. 🔵 **The supply is recency-shaped.** 13 of this pass's 17 discovered addresses were created
   within five months, and **7 of 13 ship as an agent *skill*** rather than an application. The
   packaging format of education AI changed this year.

authorities begin enforcing, while the Annex III high-risk obligations are dated **2 December
2027**. 🟢 Two passes, two source sets, one answer — so the two dates are **not a contradiction in
the sources, they are two different obligations**, and this file's EMEA child already reads them
that way. 🔴 Neither date is read from EUR-Lex by either pass, so both remain **relayed**, per

## 🆕 `p461` — **Market structure re-placed by region, with the regulatory clock as the primary driver: EMEA's high-risk education deadline MOVED to 2027-12-02, APAC made AI instruction compulsory, North America regulates state-by-state with no federal floor, and LATAM remains a declared coverage gap on the supply side.**

🔴 **Gate posture per `Gap 446-A`: `make hooks` was NOT RUN.** Nothing below is gate-attested.
🟡 **Market sizing is deliberately not restated as a point value.** Published 2026 figures for AI
in education span **~USD 7.3 B to ~USD 12.3 B** across Trendxinsights (~7.30 B), MarkWide (8.7 B),
Precedence (9.58 B), Research & Markets (10.6 B) and a HolonIQ figure (12.3 B) — a **~1.7×** spread
between vendors. 🔵 A spread that wide is itself the finding: it means sizing cannot carry an
engagement argument, and the **regulatory calendar** below can.

## Opportunities by region — 🔴 SUPERSEDED by `p463` (consolidated into the single current block above; content retained here and in `git log`)

### North America

🔵 **Regulatory shape: no federal floor, fifty state clocks.** There are **no binding federal AI
curriculum standards**; more than half of state education departments have issued **guidance**,
while binding law is sparse and specific.

| signal | detail |
|---|---|
| 🟢 Binding state law | **Idaho SB 1227** — statewide framework, local policy mandate, AI-literacy standards, data-privacy rules; **prohibits AI replacing human teachers** |
| 🟢 Hard deadline | **Ohio HB 96** — every district, community and STEM school must adopt a formal AI policy by **2026-07-01** |
| 🟡 Student-data bills | **California AB 1159** (bars training on student data unless it benefits the school); **Vermont HB 650** (annual edtech privacy certification) |
| 🔵 Guidance-only states | Illinois, Massachusetts, North Carolina, Oregon, Washington |

🟢 **Where the work is.** Ohio's passed deadline and Idaho's literacy standards create **policy-
to-implementation** work: districts that adopted a policy now need the controls the policy
promised. The shelf's permissive fit is `CAHLR/OATutor` (MIT, **271**★, UC Berkeley) for auditable
tutoring with Bayesian knowledge tracing, plus `pykt-team/pykt-toolkit` (MIT, **452**★) where a
district must *evidence* learning gain rather than assert it. 🔴 The binding constraint is
**student-data residency**, which favours self-hosted and local-model builds over API-only ones.

### EMEA

🔴 **The single most actionable regulatory fact this pass: the high-risk deadline moved.** The
**Digital Omnibus, Regulation (EU) 2026/1744**, postponed stand-alone high-risk education systems
from **2026-08-02 to 2027-12-02** — roughly **sixteen additional months** before automated
marking, admissions triage and exam proctoring must meet human-oversight and logging duties.

| EU AI Act clock | date | status |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 Emotion recognition in schools | **2025-02-02** | **already prohibited** (narrow medical/safety exception) |
| 🟡 Transparency — disclose the user is talking to an AI | **2026-08-02** | **NOT delayed** — applies to student-facing chatbots now |
| 🟢 Stand-alone high-risk education systems | **2027-12-02** | delayed from 2026-08-02 |
| 🔵 AI embedded in regulated products | **2028-08-02** | unchanged |

🔵 **Annex III scope:** systems that evaluate learning outcomes, determine the level of education a
person can access, or monitor students for prohibited behaviour during tests. 🟡 Predictive
early-warning / dropout tools are a **grey area** — classifiable as high-risk on some readings,
which is a live design risk for exactly the pyKT-style dropout work this shelf recommends.

🟢 **Where the work is.** Schools are **deployers**, not merely buyers, and carry their own
compliance duties — and the Act reaches non-EU systems whose outputs affect EU-located students.
That makes **compliance scaffolding** (audit logs, human-in-the-loop checkpoints, disclosure
surfaces) billable now, ahead of 2027-12-02, while transparency duties already bind. 🔵 EMEA-rooted
shelf rows: `openfun/richie` (MIT, **316**★, France Université Numérique), `ILIAS-eLearning/ILIAS`
(GPL-3.0, **505**★, DE/CH/AT), `artcc/freelingo` (AGPL-3.0, **208**★, self-hosted with Ollama),
`GarethManning/education-agent-skills` (**913**★, UK — 🔴 **CC-BY-SA-4.0**, share-alike).
🟢 Data-residency pressure makes **self-hosted open-weight** builds the default here.

### APAC

🔵 **Regulatory shape: no regional model; binding frameworks in some states, guidance in others.**
Binding: **South Korea** (AI Basic Act), **China**, **Vietnam**. Guidance / existing law:
**Japan**, **Singapore**, **Hong Kong**, **Australia**, **India**.

| signal | detail |
|---|---|
| 🟢 China — compulsory curriculum | AI a **compulsory subject from age six** (from Sept 2025); Beijing schools owe **≥8 hours** of AI instruction per student per year |
| 🟢 China — service filings | generative-AI filings **796** (2026-02-28) → **988** (2026-06-30) |
| 🟡 China — content labelling | AI-generated content labelling in force since **2025-09-01**; no general AI law, rules are service-specific |
| 🟢 India — compulsory curriculum | AI and computational thinking compulsory **from Class 3**, beginning the **2026–27** academic year, backed by the **IndiaAI Mission** |
| 🟡 India — labelling | amended IT Rules require AI-content labels and provenance markers from **2026-02-20** |
| 🔵 Sentiment | Ipsos Education Monitor 2026: **lower** support for banning AI in schools across Asian markets; **higher** in Australia and New Zealand |

🟢 **Where the work is.** Two national curriculum mandates landing in the same academic year is the
largest single demand event in this file: **compulsory AI instruction at national scale** needs
teacher-facing material, assessment and localisation. 🔵 APAC is also where this shelf's supply is
densest — `HKUDS/DeepTutor` (Apache-2.0, **43 537**★, HK), `Miaotofu01/Study-Mate` (MIT,
**1 218**★), `HugeCatLab/ChatTutor` (AGPL-3.0, **1 314**★), `KeWang0622/kaogong-skill` (MIT,
**204**★), `SimonsTang/feifei-companion` (Apache-2.0, **109**★, K-12),
`liuliu667/cram-engine` (MIT, **176**★), `flysheep-ai/education-skills` (MIT, **118**★),
`DAMO-NLP-SG/M3Exam` (**105**★, multilingual benchmark, Singapore).
🔴 `ECNU-ICALK/EduChat` (**975**★) is APAC's notable **unusable** asset — no grant, ~15 months stale.

### LATAM

🔴 **Regulatory shape: no unified regional framework for AI in education.** Most universities
operate in a normative vacuum, deciding in isolation.

| jurisdiction | status |
|---|---|
| 🟢 Brazil | **PL 2338/2023 approved by the Senate**; **ANPD** became an independent agency (April 2026) with minors' data **and AI** on its roadmap |
| 🟡 Mexico | **no general federal AI law**. **State of México** reformed **Art. 61** of its Education Law (April 2026) to promote responsible, graduated AI use in upper-secondary and higher education — **first state to legislate AI in the classroom**. A federal proposal would create a centralised National AI Agency |
| 🟢 In force | **Peru** and **El Salvador** have AI laws in force |
| 🟡 In progress | **Colombia**, **Brazil**, **Chile** bills in legislative passage |
| 🔵 Adoption base | **IDB documents 193 AI solutions** already operating across Latin America and the Caribbean |
| 🔵 Tiering | Mexico sits in a **second tier** with Argentina, Brazil and Colombia — technical and institutional progress, but uneven implementation and access |

🔴 **DECLARED SUPPLY GAP — written down rather than left silent.** This pass searched the
repository index in **Spanish** (`educación inteligencia artificial docentes`) and **Portuguese**
(`educação inteligência artificial professores escola`) at `stars:>15` / `>20`. **No
LATAM-originated open-source education-AI agent repository surfaced at or above 50★.** The only
substantial LATAM-rooted result was [`institutohumai/cursos-python`](https://github.com/institutohumai/cursos-python)
(**621**★, Instituto Humai, Argentina) — which is **AI curriculum content, not an agent**, and so
is not an agents-shelf row.

🟢 **What that gap means commercially, and it is not a negative.** LATAM demand is real (193
documented IDB solutions; Brazil and Peru/El Salvador legislating) while **indigenous open-source
supply is absent**. The region therefore consumes platforms built elsewhere — which is precisely a
systems-integration and localisation market rather than a build-from-local-OSS market.
🔵 `chamilo/chamilo-lms` (GPL-3.0, **1 020**★) is the LATAM-weighted LMS of record (Colombia,
Ecuador, Bolivia) and the natural integration base; `openeducat/openeducat_erp` (**LGPL-3.0**,
**899**★) fits private school networks needing SIS + ERP together. 🟡 The LGPL reading matters
here specifically: under `Gap 461-B` an index-only check would have called `openeducat_erp`
ungranted and discarded the best-fitting LATAM vertical on the shelf.

not cited. A bare "134 bills in 31 states" asserts a precision no tracker claims.

- 🟢 **California `AB 1159`** (Addis) — **signed**; bars covered operators from using student
  information collected through their products **to train a generative AI system**, and creates a
  **private right of action** for students actually harmed. 🔵 **Effective dates are staged**:
  K-12 amendments **2027-01-01**, higher-education provisions operative **2027-07-01**.

  an **AI coordinator designated by each local school system**, and an AI Education Collaborative.
- 🟢 **Oklahoma `SB 1734`** — signed **2026-05-12** (Senate **42-0**, House **89-0**), effective
  **2026-07-01**: instructional AI tools must be **educator-directed with human-in-the-loop review**.

### 🟢 The market shape EMEA was missing

🟢 **A closed national system of record is a different commercial object from a licensable
platform**, and these shelves had no row for one. ÉcoleDirecte (France) holds the record and
publishes nothing; three independent parties maintain clients under three different grants
(Apache-2.0, GPL-3.0, and an unpayloaded ISC). 🔴 **The buyer-side consequence: integration risk
and licence risk attach to the connector, and cannot be avoided by choosing a different platform.**
🔵 The same shape is already on these shelves unnamed in two other regions — SIIAU (Mexico) and
Librus (Poland) — so it is a recurring market structure, not a French peculiarity.

### 🟢 What the one novel datum is, and why a supply row is the thing that moved a region

🟢 [`yasserstudio/geoalgeria`](https://github.com/yasserstudio/geoalgeria) — 🟢 **MIT**, `LICENSE`
**1 101 B `sha256₁₂ 691e4ec845d7`**, read first-hand at `HEAD` — publishes **Algeria's education
estate** as data: **177** higher-education institutions (**MESRS**), **1 932** vocational-training
establishments (**MFEP / takwin.dz**), **11 858** schools and kindergartens (OpenStreetMap, by
cycle), all **69 wilayas**, with GPS, contacts, capacity and boarding.

🔵 **Why it matters to this file specifically.** Eight passes of regional legs have been returning
*demand* and *policy* tokens — adoption percentages, statutes, dates — and this file already holds
them all. 🟢 What it has been short of is **supply placed in a region**, and an institution registry
is the asset an engagement keys its numbers to: territorial phasing, capacity modelling, per-wilaya
cost, and a reach baseline. 🔴 **EMEA on this file has been Europe plus a gap.** This is the first
North African row, and it is permissive.

### 🔴 The asymmetry it exposes, measured across all five regions

| region | permissively-licensed national institution registry carried | state |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 EMEA | [`yasserstudio/geoalgeria`](https://github.com/yasserstudio/geoalgeria) — MIT, Algeria | 🟢 **new this pass** |
| 🟡 LATAM | [`bncc-dev/bncc-dados`](https://github.com/bncc-dev/bncc-dados) — Brazil's BNCC **curriculum** as data, CC-BY-4.0 | 🟡 curriculum, **not** an institution list |
| 🔴 North America | — | 🔴 **none.** NCES CCD / IPEDS exist as federal series; a packaged permissive form has **never been searched** here |
| 🔴 APAC | — | 🔴 **none carried** |
| 🔴 Global | — | 🔴 **none.** UNESCO UIS publishes aggregates, not institution lists |

🔴 **Four of five regions have no institution-registry asset, and three of the four have never been
searched for one.** 🟢 That is an informed gap with a next action attached, registered as
`Gap 452-D` — and it is a *cheaper* gap to close than another regional policy leg, which this pass
has just shown will nil.

🆕 `p456` 🟢 **APAC gains its first institution-level adoption rate on these shelves, and `Gap 454-F`'s narrow form closes here.** Not from the prescribed leg — 🔴 that returned enterprise-AI priorities, Singapore **financial-sector** consultations and corporate L&D (TCS/Pearson, LearnUpon, Alteryx), **zero K-12 or higher-ed policy findings**, identical to `p454`'s nil. 🟢 From a deliberately reworded leg: the **Digital Education Council *AI in Higher Education Global Survey 2026***, **45 398 responses across 35 countries**, with two APAC cuts — 🟢 **APAC faculty are the most optimistic region at 57 %** excited about AI making learning more effective and accessible, and 🔴 **APAC students show the sharpest job anxiety: 50 % expect fewer job openings by graduation against 41 % globally**. 🔴 **Opt-in through member institutions, not an audited random sample** — indicative, and stated with the figure. 🟢 **The opportunity those two numbers define is specific**: APAC faculty are the least likely cohort to need convincing and the most likely to be sold *capability*, while APAC students are the most likely to buy **employability** framing — so the same platform is positioned differently on the two sides of one institution. 🔵 The regulatory wedge is unchanged and remains the planning fact: Vietnam's **Decision `33/2026/QD-TTg`** with its three education entries and the **2027-09-01** deadline for systems already operating in education before 2026-08-15. 🔴 All relayed (`Gap 455-E`).

education**: systems providing **self-learning content from uncontrolled data sources**, systems
that **automatically assess, grade or rank students**, and systems that **monitor learner behaviour
using biometric data**. 🔵 Many listed systems are high-risk **only where the AI output is the sole
basis for a decision without meaningful human review** — so a human-in-the-loop design is a
classification argument, not merely good practice. 🟢 **Deadlines split, and the split is the
planning fact**: systems put into operation within six months of the effective date must comply by
**2027-03-01**, while systems already operating before **2026-08-15** in **health, education and
finance** have until **2027-09-01**. Obligations include **conformity assessment before
deployment** (by a registered body for some systems, provider self-assessment for others), risk
management and data-quality documentation, and **strict liability for damages**. 🟡 Regionally:
**South Korea**'s AI Framework Act took effect **2026-01-22** with a one-year penalty grace,
**Taiwan**'s AI Basic Act passed **December 2025**, **China** maintains the region's most
comprehensive binding regime (generative AI + content labelling), and **Singapore** and **Japan**
remain guidance-only. 🔴 **Still missing, and now specifically so**: no institution-level AI
adoption rate for any APAC education system was returned by any leg
(`Gap 454-F`, narrowed). 🔴 Relayed; one source dates Vietnam's instrument to July rather than
30 June, and the official text was only partially visible (`Gap 455-E`).

curriculum layer from the **national curriculum document** (BNCC, SEP, NAP), not from a

26 % figure is the opportunity**: demand is proven, governance is absent, and that gap is a
services engagement rather than a product sale.

🆕 `p456` 🟢 **The carried LATAM adoption figures are now stated as a COMPARISON rather than a level, and the comparison is the stronger fact.** `p454` shelved the Digital Education Council **LATAM 2026** cut at **92 %** of students and **79 %** of faculty engaging with AI. This pass read DEC's **global** survey — **45 398 responses, 35 countries** — at **88 %** of students and **77 %** of faculty. 🟢 **So LATAM runs ABOVE global on both sides, on the same survey programme**, which is a defensible claim in a way a bare 92 % never was. 🔵 It also sharpens the carried contradiction rather than resolving it: LATAM leads on *use* while only **30 %** of LATAM universities have a published AI policy and only **19 %** of faculty use AI-assisted assignment feedback against **50 %** of students who want it. 🔴 **That gap IS the LATAM engagement** — the region buys governance and assessment design, not adoption. 🟢 Re-confirmed this pass on an independent leg: the **UNESCO IESALC / UNU-IAS** working paper (**200 HEIs, 19 countries**, surveyed **Aug–Oct 2025**, carrying a **September 2026** publication date) across teaching, research, community engagement, administration and governance. 🔵 Public investment remains the structural constraint — **CENIA/ECLAC** put the regional average at **six times lower** than the global one — and regulatory fragmentation (Baker McKenzie) means a multi-country LATAM rollout prices per jurisdiction. 🟡 One supply note: the refused `tosuki/moodle-mcp` is a LATAM-shaped Moodle MCP server (**pt-BR** strings, university portal via Teams SSO) blocked from these shelves only for want of a `LICENSE` its own badge links to (`Gap 456-E`).

🆕 `p455` 🟢 **The LATAM adoption cell is CONFIRMED on a second independent leg, and gains its
institution-type split.** The UNESCO IESALC study launched at **Digital Learning Week 2026** with
UNU-IAS support surveyed **200 higher-education institutions in 19 countries**: 🟢 **87 %** use AI
in at least one area, **74 %** in teaching and learning, **57 %** in research — and 🔴 only
**26 %** have a **formal AI strategy**, with gaps in policies, governance structures and monitoring.
🔴 **Adoption is driven by individual academics, researchers and students rather than institutional
plans**, and **1 in 2 students reports confusion about appropriate AI use** while staff training is
comparatively common. 🟢 **By institution type**: private non-profit **84 %**, public **68 %**,
private for-profit **52 %** — and public institutions face the greater resource and capacity
constraint. 🟢 **School level, from TALIS 2024**: teachers who used AI in the previous year —
**Brazil 56 %**, **Chile 55 %**, **Colombia 53 %**, **Costa Rica 52 %** against an **OECD average
of 36 %**. 🔵 **A separate IESALC figure must not be mixed with the 26 %**: **45 %** of LAC
institutions report *formal AI guidance* against **70 %** in Europe and North America — that
measures guidance, not strategy. 🟢 **The opportunity this sharpens**: the regional deficit is
**governance**, not usage. The sellable work is an institutional AI-governance and
policy-instrumentation layer — strategy, monitoring, student-facing guidance — for institutions
that are already using AI at 87 % and cannot say under what rules. 🔴 Relayed (`Gap 455-E`).

### Global

| *"The French education ministry signed a February partnership with Mistral AI to deploy a domestic chatbot in **lycées** from the 2026-2027 academic year."* | 🔴 **No such secondary-school partnership is traceable.** What exists is **higher education** — **Amue** (the French universities' digital mutualisation agency) trialling two Mistral generative tools across **81 public establishments**, explicitly **bounded to the year 2026** as the basis for a future framework agreement, preceded by a virtual-tutor pilot in **21 establishments** from 2025 — plus a **civil-service** pilot of **10 000 state agents** from 22 October 2025, of which ~**3 000** sit in the education, culture and social ministries. **Ministry staff, not pupils.** | 🔴 **FALSE as stated** — a *school* deployment that resolves to a *university* experiment plus a *staff* pilot |
| *"The UK's Generative AI in Education framework was updated in **March 2026**."* | 🔴 **No DfE March-2026 publication is traceable.** The real dates: the product-safety expectations, first issued January 2025, were **retitled and expanded on 19 January 2026** with four new standards (cognitive development, emotional and social development, mental health, protection against manipulation); the staff-training *module 3* page carries **19 May 2026**; and the only May-2026 circular is **DE Circular 2026/02**, issued **11 May 2026** by **Northern Ireland's** Department of Education — **a different jurisdiction from the DfE**. | 🔴 **FALSE as stated**, and it conflates two jurisdictions |

a country-shaped question** (one in French, one naming the actual department). **Retire the

| 🟢 **UNESCO IESALC + UNU-IAS**, working paper on AI implementation in higher education in Latin America and the Caribbean | **200 institutions, 19 countries**, surveyed **August–October 2025** | adoption by area: 🟢 **teaching and learning 73.5 %**, research **57.0 %**, administration **34.1 %**, community engagement **20.0 %** |
| 🟢 **Digital Education Council — AI in Higher Education LATAM Survey 2026**, run with Tecnológico de Monterrey's *Institute for the Future of Education* | **30 000+ responses, 29 institutions**, published **February 2026** | 🟢 **92 % of students** and **79 % of faculty** actively engaging with AI; **94 % of faculty** expect to use AI in future teaching |

🟢 **So the 92 % is no longer a press figure.** It has a survey name, a sample size, an
institution count, a partner and a publication month — which is exactly what `Gap 444-F` asked
for, and it means this file can re-query the same two instruments every pass instead of
re-quoting a column.

⚠️ **Graded honestly: NOT read first-hand, and the reason is a host limit, not a choice.**
Both publishers were probed directly this hour and both are blocked from this host:

| source | probe |
|---|---|
| `unu.edu` | 🔴 **403** |
| `www.iesalc.unesco.org` | 🔴 **403** |

🔴 So these cells are **search-relayed**, one channel better than `p444`'s EFE relay (the
method and population are now attributed to the instrument itself) but still not a primary
read. `Gap 445-E` carries the residue, and it is a **reachability** problem now rather than a
sourcing one.

### 🟢 The reconciliation that stops this file quoting two institutional-readiness numbers

This file carries 🔴 **"< 10 % of institutions have formal guidelines and sufficient capacity"**
(UNESCO, via EFE, `p444`). This pass returned 🟡 **"only 30 % of universities in Latin America
have published policies on how to use AI."** Those look like a conflict and are not one:

| figure | what it actually measures |
|---|---|
| **30 %** | has a **published AI-use policy** |
| **< 10 %** | has a policy **AND** sufficient capacity to run it |

🟢 **The ~20-point gap between them is not noise — it is the sellable quantity.** It is the set
of institutions that have **written the policy and cannot staff it**: they have already made
the decision, already named the obligation, and lack exactly the implementation capacity a
studio sells. 🔵 That is a better qualifying question than either number alone — *"do you have
an AI policy, and who operates it?"* — and both figures are kept with their definitions
attached rather than one being dropped.

### 🟢 APAC — Korea: a national AI-education programme was reversed BY STATUTE, and that is a procurement fact

| date | step |
|---|---|
| 2024 | 🔵 **₩533.3 bn** allocated to the AI digital textbook programme in that year alone; flagship initiative of the Yoon administration |
| 2024 | 🟡 First retreat before launch: Korean and home economics **dropped**; social studies and science **deferred to 2027**, at the request of the National Council of Governors of Education |
| 1H 2025 | Pilot in English, maths and secondary computer science; national mandate replaced by **school-by-school voluntary adoption** |
| 🔴 **4 Aug 2025** | 🔴 **The National Assembly reclassified AI digital textbooks from *textbooks* to *educational materials*, effective on promulgation — removing the legal AND financial basis for school use** |
| 2H 2025 | 🔴 Adoption falls to **1 686 schools**, a **−58.8 %** drop against the first semester |
| Dec 2025 | 🔴 **State audit**: the ministry **skipped the planned pilot**; six instances of illegality or impropriety, with caution requests issued |
| Jun 2026 | 🔴 **~10 publishers sued the government for ~₩200 bn** in damages |

🔴 **The commercial reading, and it is not "Korea is a bad market".** It is that in APAC the
dominant risk to an education AI build is **procurement reversal, not model quality**. A
ministry-mandated national rollout was unwound by statute inside twelve months, and the parties
left holding the loss were **the vendors who had built to the mandate**. 🟢 So the design rule
is: build for a **voluntary, school-by-school or district-by-district buyer** who can adopt you
without a national instrument, and never let a national mandate be the revenue assumption.
🔵 The demand did not disappear — teachers moved to self-adopted tools funded by the **17
provincial offices of education**, which is the layer that kept paying. That layer is the one
to sell to.

🔵 **This is the APAC analogue of `Trend 30`** (*in North America the district, not the state,
is the layer that decides whether a build is legal*). Two regions, same structural answer:
**the sub-national layer is the buyer.**

### 🟢 North America — the state guidance count, with its instrument and date, and the spread is the finding

| as of | count | instrument |
|---|---|---|
| Jan 2026 | **31** states | Ballotpedia tracker, cited in a Carahsoft K-12 AI readiness deck (Feb 2026) |
| 🟢 **27 May 2026** | **> 35** states | 🟢 **New Mexico Legislative Education Study Committee brief** — a legislature's own document |
| Jun 2026 | **37** states **+ Puerto Rico** | Playlab policy directory |

🟢 **Monotone growth over five months, from three independent trackers: 31 → >35 → 37.** 🔵 The
spread is not a contradiction to resolve — it is the measurement's resolution, and it is the
honest way to carry the number. This file should quote **"37 states plus Puerto Rico as of June
2026, on a count that was 31 in January"**, never a bare "37 states", because the figure's rate
of change is faster than this base's own refresh interval. ⚠️ The Playlab directory itself
advises verifying status with each state department, and the count includes territories in some
trackers and not others.

### 🔴 EMEA — written down as a NIL, and one of its two apparent findings surveyed the wrong continent

The mandated leg (`AI education EMEA 2026 adoption regulation players`) returned **enterprise**
AI material and **no education-sector adoption, regulation or player facts**. Recorded as a nil
because silence reads exactly like coverage.

🔴 **And the sharper half: the one figure that looked usable is not an EMEA figure at all.**
CompTIA's IT Industry Outlook 2026 — *94 % of organizations at least somewhat likely to invest
in AI-specific training in 2026* — appears under an EMEA-titled page, and **its survey
population is US professionals only**. 🔴 Carried onto this file as an EMEA adoption rate it
would have been a region error of exactly the kind `region.py`'s closed vocabulary exists to
prevent. It is **refused**, and named here so no successor re-buys it.

🟢 **Why the nil is not a gap in coverage.** EMEA's regulatory picture was re-confirmed
independently by `p444` one pass ago and is unchanged: **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** (the
*Digital Omnibus on AI*), in force **27 July 2026**, moves the Annex III stand-alone high-risk
deadline — the one covering education — to **2 December 2027**, while transparency, GPAI, the
**AI-literacy requirement** and prohibited-practices stay on their original schedule. The
`p427` shape that pays for EMEA is a **country** leg, not a region leg; `Gap 445-F` names it.

`p437` found that `ark-forge/mcp-eu-ai-act` — then wired into `Pattern AO` — hardcoded

already passed. The leg run this hour, with no reference to that finding, returns the same

  Journal on **24 July 2026** and entered into force on **27 July 2026**.
- 🟢 It moves the **Annex III stand-alone high-risk deadline — which is the one covering
  education** — from **2 August 2026** to **2 December 2027**.

runway on Annex III conformity and **zero** runway on AI literacy and transparency. The
obligation that is *not* deferred is the one nobody is scoping, which is where the work is this
quarter.

— *regulation, adoption and players, across four countries, in one query* — and prescribed

### 🟢 APAC — India: the first education-specific adoption rate on this file for the region

| source | population | finding |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **JanAI, released September 2026** | **797 government-school teachers, four states** | 🔴 **82 % have tried an AI tool at least once; only 26 % use one regularly.** 72 % hold a positive view |
| 🟢 **CSF — BaSE 2025** | 2 500 government, aided and private teachers | **51 %** already using generative AI for teaching or school-related work |
| 🟡 CENTA 2025 | not stated in reachable material | **>70 %** using AI for lesson planning — 🟡 **reachable only through secondary citation, so NOT carried as a cell** |
| 🔵 Government data, 2023-24 | all Indian schools | **57.2 %** have computers; **53.9 %** have internet |
| 🔵 Digital Empowerment Foundation, 2025 | educators nationally | **15 %** are AI-fluent |

🔴 **The commercially decisive number is the 82/26 gap, not the 82.** Trial is nearly universal
and routine use is a quarter of it — so in India the sellable unit is **not** awareness or
access, it is **making one workflow stick**. 🔵 And the binding constraint under it is
**physical**: with 53.9 % of schools on the internet, an offline-tolerant design is a
requirement for roughly half the market, not a differentiator. 🟢 That is the clearest
commercial read this file has had for India, and it is only visible because the leg asked one
country one question.

### 🟢 LATAM — Brazil: `Gap 421-E(b)` is CLOSED, and the "conflict" was never a conflict

This file carried two readings of `PL 2338/2023` — *approved by the Senate* against *still in
consultation* — as an unresolved conflict for three passes, correctly refusing to pick one.
🟢 **Both were true, of one process in two houses:**

| date | step |
|---|---|
| **10 Dec 2024** | 🟢 Senate **approves** the bill |
| **17 Mar 2025** | Received by the Câmara dos Deputados (Ofício nº 235/2025, revision under **art. 65 CF**) |
| **20 May 2025** | Special committee installed; relator **Dep. Aguinaldo Ribeiro (PP-PB)** |
| Nov 2025 → | 🔴 **No parecer filed. Five voting dates set and missed** |
| **24 Aug 2026** | 🔴 Rapporteur postpones the vote until **after the October 2026 elections** |
| **2 Sep 2026** | Latest movement: attachment of related bills. Situação **"Aguardando Parecer"** |

🔴 **`PL 2338/2023` is NOT law and no article of it is in force.** 🟢 **What that means for a
pitch, stated plainly because this is the question clients ask:** Brazil has **no binding
horizontal AI statute** and will not have one in this legislative window. The compliance
surface for a Brazilian education engagement today is **LGPD plus sector rules**, not the AI
bill — and a proposal that prices AI-Act-style conformity work into a Brazilian deal is pricing
an obligation that does not exist yet. 🟡 The risk runs the other way: the bill's risk-tier
model puts education in the high-risk band, so a 2027-28 deal should be designed to meet it
while today's deal is not required to.

### 🔴 The band on every cell above, re-measured and unchanged

`Gap 425-A` was re-measured this pass on **two independent channels** — this container's `curl`
**and** the tool-side fetcher, which resolves names on its own stack. `eur-lex.europa.eu`,
`www.unesco.org`, `www.camara.leg.br`, `www.oecd.org`, `iesalc.unesco.org`, `www.multistate.us`
and `en.wikipedia.org` all fail; only `github.com` and `raw.githubusercontent.com` answer.
🔴 **The refusal is host-shaped, not tool-shaped** — it fails at name resolution on both stacks,
so no successor should spend a leg hunting for a client that gets through. 🔴 **Consequence,
carried unchanged: every policy and market cell on this file is `reported`, and no leg count
can raise it on this host.** The Brazilian tramitação above is a tracker reading, not
`camara.leg.br`; the India figures are survey reports, not the surveys. 🟢 Every *licence* cell
this pass published is `authoritative`, because the only reachable host is the one that serves
payloads.

### 🟡 And one number this pass did NOT buy

🔴 `Gap 436-D` is **untouched**: this file's strongest single LATAM pitch figure is still quoted
through a newspaper column. 🔵 Given the paragraph above, that is not a shortfall of effort —
the primary that would fix it (`iesalc.unesco.org`) is one of the seven hosts measured
unreachable. It cannot be closed from this host at any leg count, and recording that is worth
more than another attempt.

The regulatory channel for this industry is saturated at this base's reading depth, and a
pass that spends four legs there is buying confirmation at full price. 🟢 **`p440` spent
its remaining legs on payloads instead and the return was 62 previously-ungradeable
grants** — the trade is recorded on `repos/trending.md`.

🟡 **One analyst band is widened, not replaced.** The 2026 AI-in-education base now reads
**$9.58 B** (Precedence, to $136.79 B by 2035, 34.52 % CAGR) against the $10.6 B → $42.48 B
by 2030 (41.5 %) already on this file, and the **AI-tutors sub-segment** is sized
separately at **$2.7 B** for 2026 (Grand View, to $17.7 B by 2033, 30.5 %). 🔵 The
disagreement is again about *what counts as the market* and not about growth — and the
tutors figure being a quarter of the whole is the useful part, because it is the segment
this base's supply actually addresses. 🟡 Technavio's **66 % institution-wide
implementation** is carried as indicative, single-channel.

### 🟢 Amendment to the single `## Opportunities by region` block below — the copyleft read is now per-region, and it is new

`p440` made the GPL family's grants machine-readable, and the result reorders one
procurement argument **by region** rather than globally:

- **North America** — Canvas LMS (**AGPL-3.0**, verified `GRANT-PRESENT` this hour) is the
  integration reality in US higher ed. 🔴 The §13 network clause is now a *named,
  verified* obligation rather than a licence badge, and the draft-and-sign artefacts this
  base shelves (`canvas-grader-mcp`, `California-IEP-AI-project-kit`) sit **outside** the
  platform, which is the posture that keeps the obligation off the client.
- **EMEA** — Moodle and Chamilo are **GPL-3.0**: the obligation attaches to
  *distribution*, not to network use. 🟢 For a hosted EU deployment that is materially
  lighter than AGPL, and it pairs with the 2 December 2027 Annex III runway: build the
  conformity artefacts on a GPL-3.0 base and the copyleft question never reaches the
  hosting model.
- **APAC** — Open edX (**AGPL-3.0**) dominates the MOOC-scale tier that the region's
  national programmes buy. 🔴 §13 applies to every hosted modification, so the
  Apache-2.0 **XBlock** seam is not a stylistic preference here but the commercial one.
- **LATAM** — 🟢 **the strongest and least-known finding of this pass.** The region's
  SIS tier — `portabilis/i-educar` (Brazil), `RosarioSIS`, `openSIS` — is **GPL-2.0**,
  which **predates the network-service question and carries no §13 at all**. A hosted
  deployment on these platforms triggers no source-offer obligation to its users. 🔵 The
  same age that makes them look dated is a genuine hosting advantage, and 🔴 `p440`
  measured that an unversioned "GPL" rule would have condemned all three as carrying a
  truncated grant — the error would have fallen entirely on LATAM's supply.

## 🆕 `p434` · 2026-10-25 — **All four regional legs returned material; no nil. 🟢 `Gap 433-B` is CLOSED and the answer is the RESTRICTIVE one: NYCPS prohibits AI **development** of IEP and 504 plans outright, and the "contradiction" `p433` could not resolve was a scope distinction. 🔴 A second NA fact gates timing rather than scope — NYCPS has PAUSED all educational-software purchases. 🔵 Three of four legs confirmed rather than discovered, and are graded that way.**

🟢 **The decisive regulatory fact, and it closes a gap `p433` called "the single most
consequential open question on these shelves for a North America engagement".** The current NYCPS
*Guidance on Artificial Intelligence and Screen Time* carries a **"Red: Do Not Proceed"** tier
naming **IEP and 504 plan development**, with the operative sentence *"All special education
documents are developed by qualified professionals"*, citing **IDEA** and 🆕 **Chancellor's
Regulation A-710** (new to this file). An independent outlet states educators may not use AI for
grading, care and counselling, behavioural monitoring **or crafting IEPs or 504 accommodations**.

🟢 **The apparent contradiction was never one — it is a SCOPE distinction, and this is the part to
carry into a client conversation.** `p433` set the red tier against an enacted-policy quote
exempting IEP/504 from the moratorium and could not reconcile them. They govern different
subjects: the **red tier** addresses *staff* use of AI to **develop** the document (🔴
prohibited); the **exemption** addresses *student-facing* assistive technology delivered **under an
already-signed** plan (🟢 exempt from the 2K-8 ban). An exemption permitting *delivery* of granted
accommodations says nothing about *writing* the plan. 🔵 `compose/patterns.md` `Pattern AL` is
re-scoped accordingly: its drafting step is the prohibited act in NYCPS and human sign-off does
not cure it, while progress monitoring, the parent layer and accommodation delivery remain
sellable.

🔴 **The second NA fact, and it is a procurement one.** The NYCPS chancellor directed principals
to **pause all educational-software purchases** pending updated AI guidance. For the largest US
district the near-term motion is a no-cost pilot or a standards conversation, **not a purchase
order** — a forecasting fact, not a scope one.

🔵 **Three legs confirmed; they did not discover — recorded as such rather than re-presented as
findings.** EMEA returned the Digital Omnibus split this base resolved at `p380` (Annex III to
2 December 2027, Article 50 live since 2026-08-02) and returned 🔴 **nothing** on UK, DACH, Nordic,
Gulf or African education adoption — a **standing nil**, written down because silence reads like
coverage. APAC returned Korea's AI Basic Act (22 January 2026, MSIT treating 2026 as a pilot
period with limited enforcement), Taiwan's AI Basic Act (December 2025) and Vietnam's Decision
33/2026, all carried; 🆕 **new detail**: 46 high-risk categories across six sectors, three of them
education, and a sectoral compliance deadline of **1 September 2027** for education, healthcare
and finance. LATAM returned the Digital Education Council survey at **30 000+ responses across 29
institutions — 92% of students and 79% of faculty engaging with AI** — and UNESCO IESALC's
~200-institution, 19-country study, both carried; 🆕 **new detail**: **88% of LATAM faculty report
only "minimal" to "moderate" engagement**, and faculty adoption is **lowest in assessment uses**
(cheating detection, feedback generation).

🟢 **`Trend 31` is the synthesis of this pass's legs**: four jurisdictions on three continents have
converged on the same operative predicate — AI as the **operative basis of a learner decision
without a human able to intervene** — which makes one architecture portable across all four, and
makes NYCPS's drafting prohibition the one shape that architecture does *not* cure.

## Market size — use the range, never a single figure

| Segment | 2026 | Horizon | CAGR | Source class |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AI in Education (global) | **$6.9B – $11.4B** | $41B – $57B by 2030–2033 | 26% – 43% | Multiple market-research firms, mutually inconsistent |
| North America share | ~**36%** of global, ≈$3.68B | — | — | Aggregator stats page |
| e-Learning (global, all tech) | ~$250B | ~$450B | ~15% | Multiple |

🔴 **Never quote a single figure.** The spread between reputable firms is a factor of ~1.7
on today's size and ~1.4 on the horizon. Quote the range and name it as a range; a pitch
that cites one number is citing one vendor's model. Market-research education forecasts are
systematically optimistic — treat the low end as the planning number.

this file's standing rule, now with a wider band behind it.

EdTech venture funding was **$512M across 63 deals in Q1 2026, down 24 % year on year**.
🔴 **A market sized in the tens of billions and funded at half a billion a quarter is a
market where the buyer is an institution's operating budget, not a VC round** — which is
consistent with this file's standing read that the sellable work is compliance-driven and
budget-funded, not greenfield product. 🟡 Graded as a single secondary reading; it is the
only funding figure on this file and should be re-asked next pass rather than built on.

## The one number that should shape every pitch

**87% of Latin American higher-education institutions use AI in at least one area, and
26% have a formal AI strategy** (UNESCO IESALC, 200 institutions across 19 countries).
In Brazilian schools, **53% of administrators use AI and 22% work under any school
guideline** (Cetic.br).

That gap — adoption far ahead of governance — is the engagement. The buyer does not need
to be persuaded to adopt AI; they have already adopted it, without a policy, an audit
trail, or a human-in-the-loop story. **Globant's sellable unit in education is governed
adoption, not adoption.** It is also the one thing the copyleft platform tier cannot
supply and a permissive agent layer can.
