publishes, which is exactly why it should not be quoted as independent corroboration.

**Regulatory posture — restrictive on autonomy, permissive on assistance.** 134 AI-in-education
bills across 31 states in the 2026 session, concentrated on student-data privacy, classroom
use limits and curriculum. California **AB 1159** bars using student data to train AI models.
Oklahoma and Maryland require human oversight and **prohibit AI as the sole basis for
high-stakes decisions** about a student. Charleston County extends that to discipline,
placement and special-education determination. The federal **K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness
Act of 2026 (H.R. 8747)** advanced out of committee on 21 July 2026. Districts add their own
layer: Katy ISD bans generative chatbots for K-6 with scaffolded access to grade 12.

🔴 **🆕 `p434` — AND IN NEW YORK CITY THE IEP DRAFTING ENGAGEMENT IS PROHIBITED, NOT MERELY
CONSTRAINED.** The current NYCPS guidance's **"Red: Do Not Proceed"** tier names **IEP and 504
plan development** — *"All special education documents are developed by qualified professionals"* —
citing **IDEA** and **Chancellor's Regulation A-710**. 🔵 The IEP/504 clause in the moratorium runs
the other way but addresses a **different subject**: student-facing assistive technology under an
already-signed plan is *exempt from the ban*, which is not a permission to draft the plan. 🟢 **So
the NA special-education opportunity splits in two**: *drafting* is closed in NYCPS (and must be
treated as the base case to disprove elsewhere), while **progress monitoring against existing
goals, the parent-facing layer, and accommodation delivery** are open — and the third is
explicitly exempted. `Gap 433-B` closed; `Pattern AL` re-scoped.

🔴 **Timing, not scope: NYCPS has paused all educational-software purchases** pending updated AI
guidance (chancellor's direction to principals). In the largest US district the sellable motion
near-term is a no-cost pilot or a standards conversation. Do not forecast a close.

🟢 **`p433` — NEW YORK CITY IS NO LONGER UNCONFIRMED, AND IT IS THE LARGEST SINGLE DISTRICT
RESTRICTION IN THE COUNTRY.** This shelf carried a 🔴 **UNCONFIRMED** NYC moratorium from `p386`
to `p432` — forty-seven passes on one EdWeek sighting of a *proposal* with a feedback window to
2026-05-08. **It was enacted.** For the **2026-27 school year** NYC Public Schools is running a
**moratorium on student-facing generative AI in grades 2K-8**, announced **2026-09-02** by Mayor
Zohran Mamdani, with limited high-school use and grade-banded screen-time limits. It reaches
**~600,000 students**. **Companion chatbots are prohibited in every grade, high school included.**
Teachers keep approved AI for lesson planning and translation. Reporting also has the city
requiring **38 ed-tech vendors to strip AI features** for the younger grades. 🟡 Framing is
contested and both readings are in print: Chalkbeat calls it a *ban*, Gothamist a *one-year
pause*; the two draw on the same internal documents and have not been reconciled. **Nothing in
the engagement thesis turns on which word wins** — the teacher-facing lane is open either way, and
that is where a Globant build sits.

🔵 **The trap on this item, because it is an easy one to walk into.** NYC's guidance *does* name
**IEP and 504 plans** — as a **carve-out from the ban**, not as a prohibition: the official text
says the policy *"does not apply to assistive technology and associated screen time, provided
through a student's Individualized Education Program (IEP) or 504 Plan."* That is the **opposite
direction** from a restriction, and a careless read turns an exemption into a ban. Separately, the
**March 2026 preliminary** guidance's red tier was reported to prohibit AI for grading,
discipline, counselling and crisis intervention, academic placement, **and the development of
special-education and 504 plans**. 🟡 **Whether that plan-development prohibition survived into
the enacted 2026-27 policy is UNRESOLVED from this host** — a targeted search could not confirm
it, and the primary could not be read (see the egress note below). It bears directly on
`Pattern AL`, and `Gap 433-B` carries it.

🔴 **Egress note, so the grade on the two items above is legible.** `schools.nyc.gov` and
`beta.nyc` were both refused at this host's network policy (**403 to `CONNECT`**, confirmed in the
proxy's own failure log), so **no primary document was read for this finding**. What is 🟢 above is
corroborated across several independent outlets *and* quoted from the official guidance in search
results; what is 🟡 is single-sourced or could not be confirmed. 🔵 This base's convention is that
an unread primary is written down as unread rather than rounded up to first-hand.

**Canada** launched *AI for All* on 4 June 2026 — national AI literacy, watermarking of
AI-generated content, and a Canada Trusted AI Certification — direction-setting, not yet law.

**Demand signal.** 60% of US K-12 teachers used AI tools in the 2024-25 school year, 32% at
least weekly. Largest regional market share (~36%).

**Where Globant wins.** The binding constraint is *decision autonomy*, not capability. Every
one of these rules is satisfied by the same architectural move: an agent that **drafts and a
human that signs**. [`kaushikgunasekaren/canvas-grader-mcp`](https://github.com/kaushikgunasekaren/canvas-grader-mcp)
(MIT) already produces a draft-for-review **by default** — it is the cheapest compliant
starting point on the shelf, and it is Canvas-native, which is the dominant NA LMS.
Pair with [`CAHLR/OATutor`](https://github.com/CAHLR/OATutor) (MIT, UC Berkeley) where a
district wants an auditable, non-LLM knowledge model behind the tutoring claim.

🔴 **Gap:** the institutional-agent supply measured on these shelves remains largely
non-US; the NA opportunity is in **integration and governance**, not in sourcing NA-built
components.

#### 🆕 `p432` · 2026-10-25 — three amendments, one of them a correction

🔴 **The "134 bills across 31 states" figure is one tracker's count, not the count.** MultiState
reports 134 bills / 31 states; **FutureEd, re-read this pass, tracks 68 bills across 27 states
with 10 already enacted in 2026.** Both are current. They differ because they scope "AI in
education" differently. **Quote the range and name the tracker** — a single figure in a deck is
the thing a well-prepared buyer will challenge.

🟢 **New, dated, and more useful than a bill count: the mandates with deadlines.** Ohio,
Tennessee, Utah, Idaho, Oklahoma, Maryland and Virginia all **require local districts to adopt an
AI policy**; Ohio's deadline was **1 July 2026** and has passed. Alabama **HB 329** makes an
approved CS course including AI instruction a graduation requirement; Utah **HB 273** writes AI
into state CS standards; Idaho **SB 1227** requires privacy protections for school AI tools.
Federal: the US Department of Education finalised its **AI grant-priority rule on 13 April 2026**,
and 35–40 state education departments now publish official AI guidance (35+ per one tracker,
40 states plus Puerto Rico per another, September 2026).

🔵 **Why the mandates matter more than the prohibitions.** A district under a *policy* mandate with
a passed deadline has a procurement trigger and a named owner. That is a warmer entry than a
district merely permitted to buy.

🟢 **The new opportunity, and it is the pass's main finding: special education.** IDEA and §504
make the IEP a statutory instrument with a mandated human decision — and
[`robotros/California-IEP-AI-project-kit`](https://github.com/robotros/California-IEP-AI-project-kit)
(MIT) is a practitioner's draft-and-sign kit for exactly that, sourced from CDE material, with
"only an authorized system records the final entry" in its own README. Pair with
[`67tallchris/iep-builder`](https://github.com/67tallchris/iep-builder) (MIT, Arizona K-8,
bring-your-own-key) for a per-district deployment holding no vendor key, and
[`dougdevitre/access-to-education`](https://github.com/dougdevitre/access-to-education) (MIT) for
the parent-facing layer. See `compose/patterns.md`, **`Pattern AL`**.
🔴 **State it plainly in any pitch:** this tier has 0–1 stars per address, no second contributor
and no evaluation harness. There is no incumbent open base — which is the opportunity *and* the
risk. `Trend 28` has the full argument.

🔴 **Still missing, third pass running:** no grounded account of **Canada** or **Mexico** federal
education-AI policy. Canada's *AI for All* (4 June 2026) remains the only Canadian item on this
shelf and is still direction-setting, not law.

#### 🆕 `p435` · 2026-10-25 — four new quantities, one of them the fastest-moving governance number on these shelves

🟢 **District-level AI guidelines: 79 % in 2026, against 57 % in 2025** (2026 edtech-leaders
report). 🔵 **This is the number to lead a US pitch with**, because it says the governance
question has already been answered inside the buyer — the sale is no longer *whether* to
have a policy but whether the build satisfies the one they wrote last year.

🟢 **35+ state departments of education carry official AI guidance** as of June 2026
(advocacy tracker — treat the count as approximate, the direction as solid).

🟡 **A second legislative tracker, not a correction.** FutureEd tracks **68 bills across 27
states** in this session with **10 enacted in 2026**. 🔴 This file already carries **134
bills / 31 states** from a different tracker, and `p432` already recorded that a bill count
is *one tracker's*, never *the* count. **Both figures stand, from different denominators;
neither supersedes the other, and a proposal should cite whichever it names.**

🟢 **Federal procurement signal, dated:** the **US Department of Education finalised a rule
on 13 April 2026** prioritising federal education grants for projects that expand
understanding of AI or its appropriate and ethical use. 🔵 That is a funding lever a
proposal can be written against, and it is the first federal *money* instrument on this
shelf rather than a direction-setting one.

🟡 **Readiness still lags adoption, and the gap is the engagement.** Gallup, May 2026:
**18 %** of teachers report having received any formal guidance from their school. A House
subcommittee statement cites **70 %** of teachers not feeling prepared to use AI
effectively. RAND, December 2025: **62 %** of middle-school and college students already use
AI for homework help. 🔴 **Students are ahead of their institutions' rules**, which is
exactly the exposure the 79 % figure above is a response to.

🟢 **New supply placed here this pass:** [`mitodl/open-learning-ai-tutor`](https://github.com/mitodl/open-learning-ai-tutor)
(MIT, in production at MIT Open Learning, pedagogical engine published as arXiv:2410.03781),
[`instructure/ims-lti`](https://github.com/instructure/ims-lti) (MIT, the **Canvas vendor's
own** LTI library), [`jibberswrld/fcps-school-mcp`](https://github.com/jibberswrld/fcps-school-mcp)
(MIT, Schoology + StudentVUE + Ion for one large district) and
[`anthropics/k12-teacher-skills`](https://github.com/anthropics/k12-teacher-skills)
(Apache-2.0, four teacher skills **shipped with their evaluation framework**).

#### 🆕 `p436` · 2026-10-25 — the state-policy layer, and an explicit tracker caveat

🟡 **Scale, from policy trackers rather than from statute text**: roughly **33–35 states plus
Puerto Rico** now publish official guidance on AI in education (up from ~28 in early 2025), and
one tracker counts **134+ bills across 31 states** touching K-12 and higher education.
🔴 **Both figures are a tracker's count, not a count this base verified**, and the trackers
disagree with each other on individual bill statuses — `p432` already corrected one NA bill
count on exactly this ground, so the caveat is carried rather than discovered.

🟢 **The commercially decisive shift is from guidance to binding district obligation**, and two
dates carry it:
- **Ohio** — every district, community school and STEM school had to **adopt an AI use policy by
  1 July 2026**. A policy mandate with a deadline is a services engagement, not a memo.
- **Illinois** — **AI literacy instruction for grades 6–12**, reported effective
  **1 August 2026**. 🟡 Single-source in this pass's channel; treat as indicative.

🔵 **Where the spend follows**: the recurring obligations across these instruments are *teacher
training and professional development* (Maryland SB 720, Virginia SB 394) and *student-data
governance* — anonymisation, data minimisation, parental consent and retention (California's
January-2026 guidance), with proposals to bar student data from training models absent direct
school benefit. 🔴 **And the weak spot is vendor accountability**: most current proposals do
**not** require vendors to supply auditable records of user interactions — which is exactly the
gap the staged human-sign-off artefacts on `agents/top.md` are built to fill.

🔴 **Stated limit**: several sources in this leg are vendor or advocacy blogs, and one tracker
states plainly that as of 2026-09-07 there is **no single state-law pattern** for school AI.
Primary statute text was not read this pass.

**🆕 `p441` · 2026-10-25 — the sub-statutory layer, which is the layer an engagement actually has to satisfy.** 🟢 Two instruments new to this file:

- **Arkansas `H.B. 1958`** — public entities **including school districts, open-enrolment charter schools and postsecondary institutions** must adopt policies for authorised use of AI and automated decision tools, each policy defining permitted uses and requiring **a named authorised human employee to make the final decision**. 🟢 That is a human-in-the-loop mandate written as procurement language, and it matches the posture this base already sells (`Pattern X`, staged reviewable artefacts).
- **Illinois `S.B. 1920`** — the state board must publish K-12 AI guidance for districts and educators by **1 July 2026**, so Illinois districts are buying against guidance that is already live.
- 🔵 **Ohio and Tennessee require districts to write their own AI policies** instead of regulating centrally, which makes the *district* the compliance counterparty, not the state. 🟡 One tracker counts **134 AI-in-education bills across 31 states** (Multistate, April 2026) — carried as indicative and single-channel, as it already is on this file.

🔵 **The read for a North America engagement:** the statute tier is saturated (see the leg grade above) and the **authorised-use policy** is the artefact being bought. Deliver it as a signed-off document plus the evidence trail that produces it, not as a feature.


#### 🆕 `p444` · 2026-10-25 — the federal lever is now a GRANT PRIORITY, and it has a hole in it

🟢 The U.S. Department of Education **finalised a priority on advancing AI in education**,
published in the Federal Register in **April 2026** and **effective 13 May 2026**. It
prioritises applications that expand understanding of AI or of its appropriate and ethical use.
Its K-12 companion priorities name **age-appropriate AI and computer-science offerings** and
**embedding AI and CS into teacher-preparation programmes**.

🔵 **Where the money actually is:** teacher-preparation curriculum. The priority names it
explicitly, and it is the one deliverable a studio can build at scale without touching student
data.

🔴 **And the hole is the opportunity.** The federal rule, unlike California's state guidance,
**contains no plan to evaluate student-data privacy or equitable access**. Meanwhile **31 states
had issued K-12 AI guidance as of January 2026, most of it voluntary.** So a district buying
against the federal priority gets no privacy-evaluation framework with it, and its state
guidance probably does not mandate one — 🟢 **a privacy-and-equity evaluation layer is
unbundled, unmandated and needed**, which is the cleanest NA service shape on this file.

⚠️ Sources are secondary (trade press, compliance summaries); the Federal Register notice is the
authoritative text and has not been read first-hand.

🔴 **Mandated AI in the classroom, with no binding AI statute anywhere in the region.**

| Jurisdiction | Education mandate | Binding instrument that actually reaches AI |
|---|---|---|
| 🇦🇪 **UAE** | 🟢 AI a **nationwide K-12 curriculum requirement** (Cabinet, May 2025) — ~1 M public-school pupils, **seven strands**. 🟡 Private schools **not** required | 🔴 None horizontal. Federal AI & data authority *announced* June 2026 |
| 🇸🇦 **Saudi Arabia** | 🟢 AI **compulsory in public schools** from 2025-26. 2026 designated "Year of AI" | 🔴 SDAIA strategy/ethics **non-binding**; **PDPL** (data protection) is the binding rule |
| 🇳🇬 **Nigeria** | 🔴 No education-specific rule found | **Data Protection Act 2023**; National AI Strategy (2024) non-binding |
| 🇰🇪 **Kenya** | 🔴 No education-specific rule found | **Data Protection Act 2019**; AI strategy 2025-2030 non-binding |
| 🌍 **African Union** | — | 🔴 Continental AI strategy adopted; **no continent-wide law** |

🟢 **The opportunity this shape creates, stated as a motion.** Where the curriculum is
mandated and the statute is absent, the binding constraint is **data protection**, not AI
law — so the sellable artefact in the Gulf and in Nigeria/Kenya is a **PDPL/DPA-anchored
deployment** (local or self-hosted inference, learner data never leaving the jurisdiction, an
auditable training boundary), not an AI-Act-style conformity pack. 🔵 That is the *opposite*
posture to the EU motion below, in a region frequently sold the EU one. The offline-first,
self-hostable tier on `agents/top.md` — `kolibri` (MIT, the only MIT platform),
`Open-TutorAi` with Ollama, `rubric-desk` — is the supply that fits, and the UAE's
seven-strand curriculum is a **curriculum-grounding** target of exactly the shape
`RAG-for-NCERT` (new this pass) and the BNCC / BC / `os-taxonomy` instances already take.

either channel this pass; `evidence/arabic-supply-leg.2026-10-23-p401.tsv` holds the prior
nil and it still stands.

**🆕 `p439` — the EU leg, re-read, with one date dispute left visible.**

- 🟢 **Enforcement of the AI Act began 2 August 2026**, by the AI Office together with national
  authorities. 🟡 Targeted amendments (the "AI omnibus") were proposed **November 2025** and
  are not settled.
- 🔴 **Education is Annex III high-risk, and the scope is wider than "grading".** It reaches
  any system that **evaluates learners, assigns them to programmes, detects prohibited
  behaviour during assessment, or influences access to qualifications** — so proctoring and
  *placement* are in scope alongside marking.
- 🔴 **Extraterritorial.** Placing a product on the EU market, or deploying one that affects
  EU learners, is enough. A LATAM- or APAC-built tutor sold into an EU institution carries
  these duties.
- 🟡 **One date is disputed in this pass's own sources**: high-risk obligations phasing in
  **December 2027 – August 2028**, which likely reflects the omnibus. 🔵 Carried as disputed
  rather than resolved, because the primary is unreachable from this host.
- 🟢 Denmark's national AI strategy commits **€200 M** to R&D with education named among three
  focus areas — the clearest single funded EMEA entry point on this file.
- 🟡 Europe's adoption is repeatedly characterised as slower but **more structured and more
  durable once it lands**, with Eurostat-based commentary calling progress fragmented. A
  €2.11 B → €19.97 B (2026→2034, 32.5 % CAGR) projection appears in a commercial forecast
  naming IBM and Microsoft as leaders — 🔴 vendor forecast, not citable.


**🆕 `p437` — two new African instruments, and the supply-side gate that decides EU scope.**

🟢 **New to this file (both were at zero mentions before this pass):**
- 🇪🇬 **Egypt** — AI and programming become **mandatory subjects for first-year secondary
  students** from the current academic year. This is a second Arab-world mandate alongside
  the UAE's K-12 core subject, and it lands in the lane this base already built the
  🆕 `p401` Arabic-first watch tier for.
- 🇷🇼 **Rwanda** — AI concepts introduced into school technology curricula, reported
  alongside several other African systems. 🟡 Single-source and thin; recorded as a watch
  item, not a finding.

🔵 **The UK picture is a training gap, not a mandate gap.** No single mandated AI subject;
the existing computing curriculum (compulsory since 2014) is being updated incrementally.
The commercially relevant number is workforce capacity: **only ~21 % of state-school
teachers report formal AI training**, against private schools far more likely to hold a
whole-school AI strategy. 🟡 Sector-body sourced (Computing at School, May 2026).

🔴 **The scope gate that matters more than the deadline.** This subsection already carries
the Digital Omnibus dates correctly. What it did **not** carry, until `p437`, is
**Article 6(3)** — the only exit from Annex III classification. The exception requires one of
conditions (a)–(d) *and* no significant risk, **and it is unavailable where the system
profiles natural persons**. Knowledge tracing and adaptive learning profile learners by
construction, so:

> 🔴 **Every adaptive-learning or automated-grading product this base recommends building in
> the EU is high-risk. The Art. 6(3) escape hatch is closed for it by design.**

🟢 **Sell that, do not hide it.** It converts a vague compliance worry into a fixed,
statutory scope of work with a 2 December 2027 date, and it disqualifies competitors pitching
"lightweight" adaptive learning on an assumed exception. The executable version of this rule
is `tomdxb0004/eu-ai-act-risk-checker` `engine.js:170-186`; the engagement shape is
`compose/patterns.md` **Pattern AP**.

**Regulatory posture — the most specific in the world, and the deadline just moved.**
The 2026 Digital Omnibus, **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744**, pushed the high-risk obligation for
stand-alone education AI — **grading, admissions, exam proctoring** — from 2 August 2026 to
**2 December 2027**; systems embedded in Annex I regulated products get until 2 August 2028.

🔴 **Do not read the delay as a reprieve.** Three things bite now:
- **Article 50 transparency** applies from **2 August 2026**: disclose AI interaction, mark
  synthetic audio/image/video/text in machine-readable form.
- **Emotion recognition in educational institutions is prohibited outright** since
  2 February 2025 (narrow medical/safety exception). This kills a whole category of
  engagement-detection and proctoring product in the EU.
- **Article 4 AI literacy** has applied since February 2025; softened on 27 July 2026 to
  *taking measures* to support staff skills rather than *ensuring* a level — no mandatory
  hours, no certification.

The European Schools network has **no generative AI tool approved for system-wide use**,
with an assessment procedure still being built.

**Where Globant wins.** The December 2027 deadline is an eighteen-month runway to build
conformity *into* a grading or admissions system rather than retrofit it — the highest-value
and most defensible education engagement available anywhere right now. Sovereignty is a
first-class requirement: [`Open-TutorAi/open-tutor-ai-CE`](https://github.com/Open-TutorAi/open-tutor-ai-CE)
(BSD-3) runs local models via Ollama; [`openfun/richie`](https://github.com/openfun/richie)
(MIT) is the French public-sector MOOC front-end; [`exelearning/exelearning`](https://github.com/exelearning/exelearning)
(AGPL-3.0) is led by Spain's Ministry of Education — state-backed credibility, copyleft terms.
[`huggingface/smolagents`](https://github.com/huggingface/smolagents) (Apache-2.0) is the
EU-domiciled agent base.

#### 🆕 `p432` · 2026-10-25 — the scope carve-out is the commercially decisive update

🟢 **The Commission's May 2026 draft guidelines place tutoring, practice feedback and information
chatbots OUTSIDE the high-risk category.** High-risk remains admissions, grading, placement and
exam proctoring. This is the single most useful EMEA fact on this shelf for scoping a deal: a
tutoring pilot is **not** a high-risk system, so it does not carry conformity assessment, EU
database registration or post-market monitoring — while the grading engine beside it does. Read
as draft guidance, not settled law, and re-check before contracting.

🔴 **The deployer→provider flip, which catches integrators specifically.** A school that merely
buys a tool is a **deployer**. A school — or a studio acting for it — that **adapts** a tool for a
high-risk use can become its **provider**, inheriting the full provider obligation set: human
oversight, registration in the EU's public AI database before market, and post-market monitoring.
🔵 For Globant this is the central contractual question on every EMEA grading or admissions
engagement: *who is the provider of the adapted system?* Settle it in the statement of work.

🔵 **The deadline, re-confirmed with its publication date.** Regulation (EU) **2026/1744** moved
stand-alone high-risk education systems from 2 August 2026 to **2 December 2027**; it was
published in the Official Journal on **24 July 2026**. 🔴 Several compliance-vendor guides still
print 2 August 2026 — if a client's counsel is working from one of those, they are eighteen months
out on their own runway. Article 50 transparency (from 2 August 2026) and the emotion-recognition
prohibition in educational institutions (since 2 February 2025) are **unchanged**.

🟢 **Supply note.** The highest-traction new education address this window is EMEA-built:
[`GarethManning/education-agent-skills`](https://github.com/GarethManning/education-agent-skills)
(UK, 907 ★ / 128 forks) — 165 teacher and school-leader skills. 🔴 It is **CC BY-SA 4.0**, read
from the payload: commercial use permitted, **ShareAlike obligation on derivatives**. A derived
pack is publishable, not proprietary. `Trend 29`.

🔵 **Demand note, lightly sourced:** Spain, Portugal and Italy are aligning national AI-education
frameworks with existing digital-education strategies, and teacher training is named as the
bottleneck rather than tooling. Source is a sponsored article — directional only, not a basis for
sizing.

#### 🆕 `p435` · 2026-10-25 — `Gap 434-C` CLOSED on the per-country shape, and the region finally has adoption evidence instead of only regulation

🔴 **The standing complaint, restated so the fix is legible:** for three passes this region
was served by regulation and by **no adoption evidence**, while LATAM and APAC both had
institutional survey numbers. `p427` had already measured the mechanism — the drought was a
**query-shape artefact** — and `Gap 434-C` prescribed per-country ministry legs. 🟢 **Four
legs run, four paid.**

**🇬🇧 United Kingdom — the most quantified EMEA jurisdiction on this shelf now.**
DfE's `Using AI in education settings` support materials were **refreshed 19 May 2026** for
the 2026–27 year: four modules, a leaders' support pack, Chartered College of Teaching
assessment and webinars, **including SEND-specific material**. The product-safety document
was renamed and expanded to **`Generative AI: product safety standards`, updated 19 January
2026**, launched at the UK AI for Education Summit. Adoption and readiness: **44 %** of
teachers used generative AI in 2024–25 (mainly planning and preparation); **43 %** rate
their own AI confidence at **3 out of 10**; roughly **half of schools have no AI policy at
all** and about two-thirds no rule specific to pupil use. 🟢 **The commercial read: the
guidance exists, the training does not.** A DfE-aligned enablement engagement has an
official curriculum to teach to and a measured 43 %/50 % gap to close.

**🇩🇪🇦🇹🇨🇭 DACH — the structural finding is that there is no federal lever.**
🔴 **Education is *Länder* competence and there is no federal school AI strategy** — that is
the finding, not an absence, and it determines who a proposal is addressed to. What exists:
**KMK** adopted recommendations on handling AI in October 2024 (AI in teacher training,
clear data-protection requirements); **Digitalpakt Schule** carried roughly **€1.6 bn** to
the Länder for digital school infrastructure in 2025. Telekom-Stiftung's 2026 Trendmonitor:
regular classroom AI use is rising while **57 % of teachers feel unsure** doing it, and
teaching associations are asking the Länder for a binding legal framework. Länder
programmes to name in a pitch: **AI4Teachers** in Rhineland-Palatinate (from November 2025,
two years, Fraunhofer IAIS, a multiplier model reaching up to 100 further teachers per
trained multiplier) and **15 model schools over five years** in Bavaria. 🟢 **Sixteen
Länder, each a separate buyer, each with the same unsolved teacher-confidence problem.**

**🇳🇴🇸🇪🇩🇰🇫🇮🇮🇸 Nordic — the strongest regulatory find of the pass.**
🆕 **Norway has imposed a near-total ban on generative AI for grades 1–7 (ages 6–13)**,
announced by Prime Minister **Jonas Gahr Støre on 19 June 2026**; **grades 8–10 may use it
only under direct teacher supervision**; in force from the start of the **2026–27 school
year in late August 2026**. The stated rationale is that foundational reading, writing and
arithmetic must consolidate before AI enters, and the government is also funding **physical
books**, reversing a long shift to tablets. 🔴 **Grade: multiple consistent secondaries
(AACRAO among them), no primary read** — `regjeringen.no` and `udir.no` are unreachable
from this host, the same ceiling `Gap 433-C` records. Higher education: a peer-reviewed
content analysis of **39 documents from 12 Nordic universities** finds guidance organised
around classroom use and ethical principles, **short on worked examples for teachers and
students**, and silent on societal and environmental wellbeing; **Iceland** is the least
prescriptive case. 🔴 **Nil: no 2026 national school guidance returned for Finland, Sweden
or Denmark**, and no adoption rates for any of the three.

**🇦🇪🇸🇦 Gulf — the most aggressive jurisdiction in the world, re-dated.**
🆕 **UAE: the Cabinet approved an AI curriculum for all public *and private* schools**,
announced **2 September 2026** — data, algorithms, applications, risks and ethical use —
with **~22 000 teachers and educators** to be trained to use AI for teaching, assessment,
curriculum analysis and lesson planning. Design, Innovation and Computer Science were
**merged with AI into one subject, `Artificial Intelligence and Technology`**, across the
first three educational cycles, delivered play-first in the early years. 🟡 Sources differ
on the start year (earlier coverage says 2025–26, the September 2026 announcement describes
the full rollout) — **quote the announcement date, not the academic year.**
**Saudi Arabia:** the National Curriculum Center with the Ministry of Education, MCIT and
**SDAIA** rolled a national AI curriculum from **2025–26**, with **AI learning outcomes
written into the national student performance assessment system**, after an "Introduction to
Artificial Intelligence" pilot for third-year secondary students. 🟡 No 2026 implementation
update returned for Saudi Arabia.

**🌍 Africa (fifth leg, added beyond the prescription).**
🟢 `AI Research Foundations` — Google.org-funded, delivered with **AIMS** — trains lecturers
at **30 universities across Nigeria, Ghana, Kenya and South Africa**, running **2026–2028**,
teaching an adapted Google DeepMind curriculum; the **FATE Foundation AI Upskilling
Programme 2026** targets lecturers, teaching assistants and students in the same four
countries. In Nigerian secondary schools **Experience AI** (Raspberry Pi Foundation +
Google DeepMind) continues into 2026 after equipping **1 142 public-secondary educators
across five south-western states** in year one — 🟡 sourced from a sponsored post, so read
the figure as the programme's own claim. 🔴 **Explicit nil: no ministry-level AI curriculum
returned for Kenya or South Africa**, and no national kindergarten-to-tertiary AI policy for
the group. 🔵 **The shape of the African opportunity is therefore *university lecturer
enablement*, which is who the funded programmes are already reaching** — not ministry
curriculum work, which has no instrument to attach to yet.

now the **largest** regional supply block on these shelves rather than the thinnest.

🟢 **The commercial consequence, stated plainly:** an EMEA engagement no longer has to open
with "we will build you a platform." It can open with the client's **own ministry's code**
(Udir, DfE, Oak) and a **permissive connector to the platform they already run**.

**🆕 `p441` · 2026-10-25 — the generic regional leg returned nil for the FIFTH consecutive pass, and the per-country leg paid in one request.** 🟢 `Gap 434-C` prescribed exactly this shape and it now holds twice: **in EMEA the region is not the unit of inquiry; the ministry is.**

- **Austria — `Schoolpackage AI`** 🟢 **new to this file.** The Federal Ministry of Education, Science and Research promotes AI tools, generative AI among them, through **pilot programmes in selected schools** (OECD.AI policy navigator). 🔵 A pilot programme is a procurement shape: scoped, funded, time-boxed, and it names the schools.
- **Slovakia** — Responsible Use of AI in Education plan **2025–2027**, with national education programmes updated to include AI from the **2026/2027 school year**. 🟡 One secondary source dates the curriculum rollout to 1 September 2026; the ministry's own documents are the primary and were not reachable from this host (`p433`'s standing ceiling).
- **Italy** — ministerial **Guidelines for the introduction of AI in educational institutions**, tied to the national AI strategy 2024–2026. **Türkiye** — AI in Education Policy Document and Action Plan **2025–2029** (AI literacy, materials, assessment, data-driven school management).
- 🔵 **Cross-country comparator:** the **CIDREE / NCCA curriculum audit** (2nd edition, 2025, led by Ireland's NCCA) compares national AI curriculum responses across member organisations — a single document that places a bid against its neighbours.

🔵 **The read for an EMEA engagement:** the EU AI Act gives the *deadline* (Annex III education to **2 December 2027**) and the national programme gives the *buyer*. Ask which programme funds the pilot before pricing the conformity work.


#### 🆕 `p444` · 2026-10-25 — the date is settled, and the UNDEFERRED obligations are the live work

🟢 **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744** (*Digital Omnibus on AI*), in the Official Journal **24 July
2026**, in force **27 July 2026**, moves the **Annex III high-risk deadline covering education
from 2 August 2026 to 2 December 2027**. Requirements unchanged; date moved.

🔴 **Not deferred, and therefore the live engagement:** transparency duties, GPAI provider
obligations, the **AI-literacy requirement**, and the prohibited-practices ban. An institution
running admissions, assessment or proctoring has ~14 months on conformity and **no runway at all**
on literacy and transparency.

🔵 This independently re-confirms `p437`'s correction of `ark-forge/mcp-eu-ai-act`, which
hardcoded `2026-08-02` and told education clients their deadline had passed. The scoping gate
remains `Pattern AP`, and `Article 6(3)`'s profiling override still lands knowledge-tracing
architectures (`pykt-toolkit`, `OATutor`) in high-risk **by construction**.

### APAC

**🆕 `p447` — NIL on discovery.** 🔴 Vietnam's education high-risk register, Korea's **AI Basic
Act** in force **22 January 2026** with a lenient first year and a one-year penalty grace,
Taiwan's **AI Basic Act** (December 2025) and Singapore's agentic-AI framework as
**guidance rather than law** all re-returned, and **all are already on this file**. 🔵 The one
structural line worth restating because it survives every re-read: APAC is the region where
**the statute and the enforcement date are different questions**, so an APAC engagement prices
the **grace period**, not the commencement date.

**🆕 `p446` — NIL on discovery; the regulatory picture re-confirmed on three jurisdictions.**
🔴 Already carried, all of it: Vietnam's education high-risk register (high-risk only where the
AI output is the **sole basis** for a decision without meaningful human review; systems running
before **15 Aug 2026** comply by **1 March 2027**), Korea's **AI Basic Act** in force
**22 January 2026** with a one-year penalty grace treating 2026 as a pilot, and Taiwan's AI
Basic Act (**December 2025**). 🔵 **The sole-basis test is the recurring commercial hinge across
this region** — it is satisfied by the draft-and-sign boundary this base shelves
(`kaushikgunasekaren/canvas-grader-mcp` produces a draft for human sign-off by default;
`jpmanson/siu-guarani-mcp` is read-only by construction), so the oversight artefact, not the
connector, is the deliverable. 🔴 **Named market-report player lists for the region (Google,
Microsoft, IBM, Pearson, Byju's) were returned and are NOT carried** — a commercial vendor list
with no method behind it is not a market map.

**🆕 `p445` — 🔴 Korea: a national AI-education programme was reversed BY STATUTE, and this is a procurement fact, not a technology one.**

| date | step |
|---|---|
| 2024 | 🔵 **₩533.3 bn** allocated to the AI digital textbook programme in that year alone |
| 2024 | 🟡 Korean and home economics **dropped**; social studies and science **deferred to 2027** |
| 1H 2025 | Pilot in English, maths, secondary CS; national mandate replaced by **voluntary adoption** |
| 🔴 **4 Aug 2025** | 🔴 **National Assembly reclassified AI digital textbooks from *textbooks* to *educational materials***, removing the legal and financial basis for school use |
| 2H 2025 | 🔴 Adoption falls to **1 686 schools** — **−58.8 %** against the first semester |
| Dec 2025 | 🔴 **State audit**: the planned pilot was **skipped**; six instances of illegality or impropriety |
| Jun 2026 | 🔴 **~10 publishers sued the government for ~₩200 bn** |

🔴 **The reading: in APAC the dominant risk to an education AI build is PROCUREMENT REVERSAL,
not model quality.** A ministry-mandated national rollout was unwound by statute inside twelve
months and the loss landed on **the vendors who had built to the mandate**. 🟢 So: design for a
**voluntary, school-by-school or district buyer** who can adopt without a national instrument,
and never make a national mandate the revenue assumption. 🔵 Demand did not disappear —
teachers moved to self-adopted tools funded by the **17 provincial offices of education**, and
that is the layer still paying.
🔵 **This is the APAC analogue of `Trend 30`:** two regions, same structural answer — **the
sub-national layer is the buyer.**

**🆕 `p442` — 🟢 two new instruments, and both are RUNWAY rather than posture.**
🟢 **Japan: draft national curriculum guidelines released 31 August.** AI and information
literacy become **core content**; schools may reduce class hours by up to **15 %** to make
room; **information classes begin 2028**; **full elementary introduction is fiscal 2030**.
🔵 Read against this file's standing measurement — *MEXT's school guideline is still Ver. 2.0,
dated 2024-12-26*, with `mext.go.jp` unreachable from this host — the correct statement is
that **the guidance tier has not moved and the curriculum tier has**. Secondary-sourced, and
marked so. **Commercially: a Japanese engagement signed in 2026 has a 2028 delivery date it
can be held to, and that is a planning horizon no other APAC jurisdiction on this file
publishes.**
🟢 **Korea: the 2026 University AI Basic Curriculum Development Support Project** selects
**20 universities** to build AI-literacy courses for students in **every major**. 🔵 Note the
tier: Korea's *school* AI-textbook programme collapsed (adoption below 30 %, official status
stripped — already on this file), while the **tertiary** channel is being funded. For a vendor
that is a redirection, not a retreat.
🟢 **India: the delivery vehicle is named for the first time here — NISHTHA**, the teacher-training
programme carrying the Class-3 AI mandate to **10 M+** educators. 🔵 The mandate was already
carried; **the programme is the thing an engagement actually plugs into** (`Trend 39`).
**🆕 `p439` — the region now has the world's second comprehensive AI law in force, and the one
jurisdiction that names education as high-risk with a dated deadline.** All readings
secondary; see the egress note at the foot of this block.

- 🟢 **South Korea's AI Basic Act took effect 22 January 2026** — the second comprehensive AI
  law anywhere, after the EU AI Act. Any Korean engagement is now a *regulated* engagement.
- 🔴 **Vietnam is the sharpest commercial clock on this file.** Its high-risk list **covers
  education by name** (automated assessment, behavioural monitoring), with a qualifier that
  matters: many listed systems count as high-risk **only where the AI output is the sole basis
  for a decision** — 🟢 which makes draft-and-sign a *compliance strategy*, not merely good
  practice. Compliance is due **1 March 2027**, with systems in education (plus health and
  finance) **already operating before 15 August 2026** getting a further six months.
- 🟡 **Everyone else is voluntary, and the spread is the point**: Japan guidance-based,
  Singapore voluntary frameworks plus testing tooling (AI Verify), Australia's National AI
  Plan keeping mandatory guardrails **paused**, and 🔴 China strictest of all — mandatory
  registration with substantial penalties.
- 🟢 **Public opinion is not uniformly pro-adoption, and it runs inversely to the mandates.**
  Ipsos Education Monitor 2026: support for **banning** AI in schools runs **23 % (Indonesia)
  → 31 % (South Korea)**, with Australia and New Zealand **higher still**. 🔵 For a studio
  that is a change-management line item in ANZ engagements, not a marketing problem.
- 🟢 **Sovereign models are the region's structural feature**, and the reason an APAC
  deployment should be model-pluggable by default: India's **Sarvam AI**, Malaysia's **ILMU**,
  Indonesia's **Sahabat AI**, Singapore's **SEA-LION**, South Korea's **HyperCLOVA X Think**,
  Japan's **NTT Sarashina**, Taiwan's **TAIDE**. 🔵 Provider indirection is not an
  optimisation here; it is what makes one codebase sellable in seven markets.
- 🟢 **And APAC is where this pass's new supply actually came from**: 8 of the 11 shelvable
  addresses are APAC-authored (Chinese gaokao and NETEM coaches, Indian NCERT grounding,
  engineering-exam tutors). 🔵 The tail of the discovery channel is disproportionately an APAC
  tail — a sourcing fact worth knowing when staffing a build.


**🆕 `p438` — the one leg that discovered this pass: three quantities at zero prior mentions.**

🟢 **1. Singapore's agentic-AI governance instrument, which is the one APAC artefact that
speaks directly to what Globant ships.** IMDA and the AI Verify Foundation co-published the
**Model AI Governance Framework for Agentic AI**, `v1.0` launched **22 January 2026**, revised
in **May–June 2026** (🟡 sources disagree on **20 May** vs **5 June**; the conflict is carried
rather than resolved, because the IMDA primary is unreachable from this host — see the ceiling
measurement in `p438`'s block above). It keeps four dimensions — bound the risk up front, keep
a human accountable, implement technical controls, enable end-user responsibility — and the
May–June revision adds **system complexity** and **use of third-party solutions** as risk
factors. 🔴 **It is voluntary: no penalty clause, no registration, no enforcement mechanism.**
🔵 Separately, Singapore's Cyber Security Agency published *Securing Agentic AI*, an addendum
to its AI-systems guidance, in **June 2026** — also non-mandatory.
🟢 **Why this is commercially decisive and not trivia**: it is the first framework in APAC
whose unit of governance is **the agent**, not the model, and *"use of third-party solutions"*
is precisely the row a Globant-assembled tutor stack occupies. An engagement in Singapore can
be sold against a named, dated, four-dimension checklist instead of against general AI
principles — and the fact that it carries no penalty is an argument for adoption speed, not
against it.

🟢 **2. The adoption gap inside APAC, measured on one channel.** Microsoft's **June 2026**
reading puts **Singapore first in Asia at 64.3 %** of working-age people using AI, against
**Japan at 24.7 %** — a **2.6× spread inside the same region**. 🔵 Carried as a single
secondary reading of one vendor's diffusion measure, with its denominator stated
(*working-age population*, not students or staff), because this file's standing rule is that
an adoption percentage without a denominator is unusable.

🟢 **3. Singapore's supply-side targets**, which is the capacity half of the same story:
**National AI Strategy 2.0** triples the AI-talent target to **15 000**, and the country
carries **70+ corporate AI Centres of Excellence**. 🔵 Japan's **National AI Basic Plan**
(December 2025, already carried here) sets measurable targets against exactly the usage gap
figure 2 names.

🔵 **Graded as confirmation, not discovery, and listed so a successor does not re-buy the
leg**: the Ipsos *Education Monitor 2026* school-AI-ban support series (India **26 %**,
Singapore **28 %** — down from 37 % — Japan and Malaysia **29 %**, South Korea **31 %**) is
already on this file; China's and Singapore's *AI in Education* policy guidelines, China's
service-specific rules with the AI-content labelling duty from **1 September 2025**, Japan's
penalty-free AI Promotion Act, India's absence of an AI-specific statute with synthetic-content
labelling from **February 2026**, and the `AI Verify` testing toolkit all re-returned and are
all carried.

🟡 **The opportunity this adds for an APAC engagement**: a *"governed agentic deployment"*
offer, scoped to the IMDA four dimensions and priced for a market that has the strategy and
the budget but, on figure 2, a usage base far below Singapore's outside the city-state. The
buyer is the ministry or the institution, the artefact is the framework's own risk-assessment
step, and the differentiator is that the stack's third-party components are each cleared on
the three-column read now standing on `repos/foundations.md`.


**🆕 `p437` — confirmation across the board.** The APAC leg returned Korea's AI Framework Act
(in force 22 January 2026, with 2026 operating as a pilot year under a one-year penalty grace
period), Vietnam's binding AI law naming **education** among its high-risk sectors for
automated assessment and behavioural monitoring, and Taiwan's AI Basic Act of December 2025 —
🔵 **all already carried on this file** (Vietnam at 32 mentions, Taiwan at 5). The named
commercial players returned by the leg (Google, Microsoft, IBM, Pearson, Byju's) are a single
vendor report's list and add nothing to this base's market map. 🟡 Recorded as a third
consecutive confirming APAC pass.

**Regulatory posture — the widest divergence of any region; there is no "APAC approach".**
- **South Korea:** AI Basic Act in force **January 2026** — the region's most binding
  framework, with explicit high-impact-system obligations.
- **Vietnam:** the most education-specific rules anywhere. A decree names **automated
  assessment and behavioural monitoring** as high-risk; compliance due **March 2027**, with
  six extra months for systems already running before August 2026.
  🆕 `p434` **resolved to the instrument and the date.** The decree is **Decision
  33/2026/QĐ-TTg**, which took effect on **15 August 2026**, issued under **AI Law
  134/2025/QH15** (itself in force since 1 March 2026) and **Decree 142/2026/NĐ-CP**. It lists
  **46 high-risk categories across six sectors, three of them education**. 🟢 **The "six extra months" resolves to a stated
  sectoral deadline: 1 September 2027 for education, healthcare and finance** — so this file's
  earlier arithmetic was right and now carries the date in the regulation's own terms.
  🔵 **The predicate matters more than the date for a build:** the designation bites where the
  output is used **as the official result** of learner evaluation, and it requires human
  supervision, control **and the ability to intervene while the system operates** — the
  strictest of the four jurisdictions in `Trend 31`, and therefore the one to build to.
- **China:** mandatory model. **Japan, Singapore:** voluntary guidance. **Australia:** still
  developing.

**Demand signal.** Ipsos Education Monitor 2026: support for *banning* AI in schools is low
across most Asian markets — 23% Indonesia to 31% South Korea — and notably **higher in
Australia and New Zealand**. Sovereign model programmes are live: Singapore's SEA-LION,
India's Sarvam AI, Japan's NTT Sarashina.

**Where Globant wins.** A single regional product is the wrong shape here — the compliance
surface differs per jurisdiction. The right architecture is **one agent core with pluggable
policy nodes**, so Korea's high-impact duties and Vietnam's high-risk classification are
configuration, not forks. [`pykt-team/pykt-toolkit`](https://github.com/pykt-team/pykt-toolkit)
(MIT) is APAC-built and is the strongest open knowledge-tracing base;
[`satvik314/educhain`](https://github.com/satvik314/educhain) (MIT) covers content
generation. Pair with a sovereign model where procurement demands it. Low ban-sentiment in
SE Asia plus Vietnam's dated deadline makes **Vietnam the clearest near-term APAC entry**.

#### 🆕 `p432` · 2026-10-25 — the largest curriculum mandate in the world, and the clearest cautionary tale

🟢 **India is now the region's biggest single opportunity, with a date.** From the **2026-27
academic year, AI and computational thinking become mandatory from Class 3** across government and
private schools, backed by the IndiaAI Mission. That is a curriculum mandate at national scale
with an immediate need for teacher capability, in-language content and assessment — not a pilot.
🔵 Regulatory posture remains light: AI-generated content sits inside the existing intermediary
framework, and in **July 2026** the government signalled it may pursue dedicated AI legislation.
The permissive, APAC-built bases on these shelves are the natural starting point —
[`pykt-team/pykt-toolkit`](https://github.com/pykt-team/pykt-toolkit) (MIT),
[`satvik314/educhain`](https://github.com/satvik314/educhain) (MIT),
[`panaversity/learn-agentic-ai`](https://github.com/panaversity/learn-agentic-ai) (MIT) — and
`compose/patterns.md` `Pattern AG` already prices the India engagement.

🔴 **South Korea is the cautionary tale, and it is now a fact rather than a sentiment.** The
planned **AI textbooks saw adoption below 30% by March, and the National Assembly subsequently
stripped them of official textbook status** after unions argued the rollout had outrun teacher
preparation. This is `Trend 4` (restriction as a real countertrend) with a government-scale
example: the mandate was not defeated by capability or by law, but by **teacher readiness**. Any
APAC deck that leads with capability and treats enablement as a footnote is arguing the losing
side of a case already decided in Korea.

🔵 **Amendment to this shelf's Korea reading.** This base recorded the AI Basic Act (in force
**22 January 2026**) as "the region's most binding framework". Still true on paper — but **MSIT has
indicated 2026 will effectively be a pilot year with limited enforcement**. The obligation is real
and the enforcement is deferred: build to it, do not panic-price it.

🟢 **Supply shift worth noting.** Both high-traction new learner-facing agents this window are
APAC-built and Chinese-first: [`Miaotofu01/Study-Mate`](https://github.com/Miaotofu01/Study-Mate)
(MIT, 1 183 ★) and [`Li-Evan/Bloom`](https://github.com/Li-Evan/Bloom) (MIT, 292 ★, self-hostable
*and* a harness skill). `Trend 14` — APAC as net exporter of permissive education AI — holds for
another window.

🔴 **Declared gap:** no education-specific AI rules found for **China** or **Japan** this pass.
China's regime (mandatory, service-type-based, with content labelling) and Japan's
(voluntary, promotion-without-penalties) are general-purpose. Absent, not unexamined.

#### 🆕 `p435` · 2026-10-25 — India's mandate now has a floor *and* a ceiling, and two ministries returned nothing

🟢 **India, CBSE — the largest curriculum mandate in the world, dated to its own release.**
A **computational-thinking and AI curriculum for Classes 3–8** was presented by the
Education Minister on **1 April 2026** (PIB release), prepared with **IIT Madras, IIT
Gandhinagar and Azim Premji University** and approved by **NCERT**, for the **2026–27**
session. 🆕 **And the ceiling:** AI + computational thinking becomes **compulsory from Class
9 in the 2027–28 session, with first board examinations in 2029.** 🔵 **That pair of dates
is the planning window** — content and teacher enablement for Classes 3–8 are buyable now;
the assessment-grade work has a fixed 2029 deadline attached to a board exam, which is the
hardest commitment date on any of these shelves.

🔵 India's own School Education Secretary places the country alongside **China, South Korea,
Finland, Estonia and Singapore** as having introduced AI in schools — useful framing in a
pitch, and 🟡 a passing comparison rather than evidence about those systems.

🔴 **Explicit nil, on the prescribed shape: nothing returned for China's Ministry of
Education or Japan's MEXT.** Both were queried by ministry name alongside India and neither
produced an education AI instrument this pass. 🔵 `p427`'s lesson applies and is recorded
here for the successor: the leg that pays is the **instrument's own name** in the
jurisdiction's own language, not the country's name in English. Carried as part of
`Gap 435-C`.

#### 🆕 `p436` · 2026-10-25 — 🟢 **`Gap 435-C` CLOSED. Both legs re-run in language, both paid on the first try, and the two systems have DIVERGED**

🔵 **The prescription was exact and it worked**: query the instrument in the jurisdiction's own
language. `p425`, `p427`, `p435` and now `p436` all record the same lesson — **the query shape
was the defect, never the jurisdiction.**

🟢 **China — 《"人工智能+教育"行动计划》, and it is the most committing education-AI instrument
on any of these shelves.**
- **Document number `教科信〔2026〕1号`**, signed **2 April 2026**, published ~**10 April 2026**.
- 🔵 **Five ministries jointly**, which is the part that matters commercially: 教育部 (Education),
  国家发展改革委 (NDRC), 工业和信息化部 (MIIT), 科技部 (Science & Technology) and
  **国家数据局 (National Data Bureau)**. A plan co-signed by the data regulator and the
  industrial-policy ministry is a procurement signal, not a curriculum memo.
- Issued under the **《教育强国建设规划纲要（2024—2035年）》** and the State Council's
  **《关于深入实施"人工智能+"行动的意见》**.
- **K-12:** continue refining the **《中小学人工智能通识教育指南》**; bring AI education
  **fully into local curriculum systems** (推动人工智能教育全面纳入地方课程体系) with per-stage
  objectives, content and **class-hour requirements**; encourage cross-disciplinary teaching and
  extend AI into after-school services and study-practice activities.
- **Teachers:** 🆕 **AI is to be written into the teacher-qualification examination and
  certification** (将人工智能纳入教师资格考试和认证内容) — the single most durable demand signal
  in the document, because it makes AI competence a licensing condition.
- **Equity:** strengthen K-12 AI education bases and **support rural and remote schools** in
  running AI courses off the national platform.
- **Build items named:** 智能学伴 (intelligent learning companions) and dynamic learning-path
  optimisation.

🟡 **Japan — the finding is the NON-event, and it is a real datum rather than a nil.**
- MEXT's school guideline is **still Ver. 2.0, dated 2024-12-26** — the revision of the
  provisional Ver. 1.0 (July 2023), addressed to teachers and boards of education.
- A direction toward **Ver. 2.1** was presented to the **中央教育審議会 initial-and-secondary
  education digital-learning-foundation special committee on 2026-06-30**, and an **August-2026**
  MEXT deck still instructs schools to follow the **December-2024** text.
- Standing principles: humans retain final decisions, and information literacy is built for a
  world that contains generative AI. Higher education and technical colleges sit under a
  **separate** MEXT notice.
- 🔵 **So the two largest APAC school systems have diverged inside one year**: China mandating
  through curriculum and teacher licensing, Japan holding a two-year-old voluntary guideline
  whose own update has been in committee since June. **For a pitch, that is the APAC slide** —
  the same region needs a compliance story in one country and an enablement story in the other.

🔴 **Citation limit, stated because it decides whether this is usable in a deliverable.** Every
government primary for both legs is **unreachable from this host** — `moe.gov.cn`,
`mext.go.jp`, `nda.gov.cn` and `chsi.com.cn` all fail DNS/proxy (`curl` run this pass, `Gap 56`
class). 🟢 The document number, the five issuing bodies, the 2026-04-02 signing date and the
quoted Chinese clauses are **consistent across ~10 independent mirrors**, among them the
National Data Bureau's republication, two municipal education bureaus and two universities.
🔴 **That is strong secondary evidence and it is still secondary: do not present either
instrument as primary-sourced law.** → `Gap 436-C`.

🟢 **Vietnam, unchanged and still the sharpest APAC compliance story:** **Decision
33/2026/QĐ-TTg** under Law 134/2025/QH15 and Decree 142/2026/NĐ-CP — 46 high-risk
categories across six sectors, three of them education, with sectoral compliance due
**1 September 2027**. 🆕 **New supply placed in that jurisdiction this pass:**
[`zaikaman/SGU-Academic-MCP`](https://github.com/zaikaman/SGU-Academic-MCP) (MIT), a

(MIT) reads **NEIS**, the national education information open-data portal — the APAC

reconciled, so records are *as reported*, not *as current*.

**🆕 `p441` · 2026-10-25 — one new instrument, and one capability figure that reframes India's mandate.**

- **Singapore — `AI for Fun`** 🟢 **new to this file.** Elective **5–10 hour** AI-literacy workshops for primary and secondary schools under the existing **Code for Fun** programme, running from 2025. 🔵 Named because it is the delivery vehicle: Singapore's regulatory posture (IMDA / AI Verify, voluntary) is already on this file and is not what a school actually buys.
- 🔴 **India's mandate meets its own infrastructure.** The Class 3–8 AI requirement from the **2026–27** session (extending to Classes 9–10 in **2027–28**) is already carried here; what is new is the ground state reported alongside it: **64.7 % of schools have computers and 63.5 % have internet connectivity**. 🟢 That gap is the engagement. An offline-first or low-bandwidth delivery path is not a nice-to-have in this market — and this base already shelves the component for it (`jupyterlite/jupyterlite`, BSD-3-Clause, kernel in the browser, verified `GRANT-PRESENT` 2026-10-25).
- 🟡 **China's timing remains contradictory across sources** (compulsory from September 2025 in one, Beijing's ≥8 hours per year in another). The five-ministry action plan already on this file is the primary-adjacent anchor; the contradiction is recorded, not resolved.


#### 🆕 `p443` amendment — India: trial is near-universal, routine use is a quarter of it

🟢 **The first education-specific adoption rate this file carries for APAC** (JanAI, September
2026, **797 government-school teachers across four states**): 🔴 **82 % have tried an AI tool,
only 26 % use one regularly**, 72 % positive. Corroborated at a wider base by CSF **BaSE 2025**
(2 500 teachers, **51 %** already using generative AI for school work). 🟡 CENTA's **>70 %** is
secondary-cited only and is not carried as a cell.

🟢 **The opportunity this reframes.** India's gap is **not** awareness, access or attitude — it
is **habit**. The sellable engagement is making *one* teacher workflow stick (lesson prep,
differentiation, marking), instrumented so the 26 % can be moved, rather than another pilot
that raises the 82 %. 🔵 **And the binding constraint is physical, not pedagogical:** **53.9 %**
of Indian schools had internet and **57.2 %** had computers in 2023-24, so offline tolerance is
a requirement for about half the market. 🟢 That makes **Kolibri** (MIT, offline-first, on
[`repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md)) the structurally correct base for Indian
public-sector work, and it pairs with the **Grade-3 AI curriculum mandate from 2026-27** and
NISHTHA teacher training already recorded in this section. 🔵 Cell band: `reported`
(`Gap 425-A`).


#### 🆕 `p444` · 2026-10-25 — India writes AI into the compulsory curriculum, and the delivery constraint is the opportunity

🟢 **India's Ministry of Education has made AI and Computational Thinking a mandatory curriculum
component for Classes 3–8 from the 2026–27 session, extending to Classes 9–10 in 2027–28.** A
CBSE expert committee chaired by **Prof. Karthik Raman (IIT Madras)** is designing it, with
**NCERT** reviewing the draft. CBSE's published structure recommends **50 hours annually for
Classes III–V and 100 hours for Classes VI–VIII**, with teacher handbooks and assessment
frameworks.

🔴 **Two constraints, both of them billable.** (1) **Scale of teacher training:** the Education
Secretary has put the task at orienting **over one crore teachers**. (2) **Connectivity:**
roughly **one third of Indian schools lack internet**, and the framework was built around CBSE's
urban, English-medium, well-equipped context.

🟢 **The matching shelf row already exists:** `Kolibri` (MIT, offline-first) is on
`verticals/solutions.md` precisely for the no-internet case. 🔵 **Offline-first AI-literacy
delivery plus crore-scale teacher training is the single largest education engagement shape on
this file in any region.**

⚠️ **One figure does not reconcile and is published as a conflict, not averaged:** the
CBSE-affiliated school count is reported as **32 900** by one outlet and **~26 000** by an
independent analysis — the latter noting CBSE is **under 2 % of India's 14.71 lakh schools**.
Either way the mandate's direct reach is a small fraction of Indian schooling, and the state
boards are the real denominator (`Gap 444-G`).

- 🔴 **87 % adoption / 26 % strategy.** The UNESCO **IESALC** and **UNU-IAS** study, launched

- 🟢 **That is the clearest consulting mandate on this entire file**, and note what it is

**🆕 `p437` — two new governance instruments, and they are the mechanism the 26 % gap was missing.**

This file's strongest number is the LATAM governance gap — widespread adoption against
**26 % of institutions holding a formal AI strategy** (UNESCO IESALC). 🔵 Re-confirmed this
pass. 🟢 **What is new is the regulatory machinery now appearing underneath it**, both at zero
mentions on this file before `p437`:

| 🇧🇷 / 🇨🇴 | instrument | why it is commercially relevant |
|---|---|---|
| 🇧🇷 **Brazil** | **ANPD Pilot Regulatory Sandbox** for AI and data protection, called in June, **running to December 2026** | A data-protection authority supervising live AI pilots is the closest thing in the region to a conformity route. 🟢 A sandbox cohort is a reference-able engagement with a regulator in the room — and the window closes this December |
| 🇨🇴 **Colombia** | Proposed comprehensive AI law creating the **National Council for AI (`CON-IA`)**, attached to the Office of the President | 🟡 Still a proposal, but it names the counterparty a national AI-in-education programme would be sold *through*. Colombia, Peru and Paraguay are all drafting instruments that explicitly reach ethical use **in education** |

🟡 **Mexico, stated precisely so it is not over-read:** reforms approved in April 2026 remain
**pending publication**, and they target AI-generated sexual content and digital manipulation.
🔴 They impose **no direct obligations on AI developers or platforms** — so they do not create
an education compliance market, and should not be pitched as if they did.

🔵 **The structural read.** LATAM's gap is no longer purely a capability gap. Brazil has a
supervised sandbox with a deadline and Colombia is building a central council, while 74 % of
institutions still lack a strategy. 🟢 **The sellable artefact is the institutional AI
strategy itself** — governance scaffolding, not a model — and it is now the one region where
a regulator is actively inviting participation rather than only publishing guidance.

**Regulatory posture — fragmented and mostly non-binding, converging on a risk taxonomy.**
Uruguay became the first Latin American country to sign the Council of Europe Framework
Convention on AI and Human Rights (2025). Colombia set national AI policy via **CONPES 4144**
(February 2025). Mexico has a 2024 federal bill proposing an oversight authority **and no
regulator to enforce it**. UNESCO launched a regional **Observatory on AI in Education for
LAC** in April 2026. 🟢 **🆕 `p434` — the adoption number is confirmed and its DEPTH is now measured, which changes
what to sell.** The Digital Education Council's LATAM survey (**30 000+ responses, 29
institutions**) reports **92% of students and 79% of faculty** engaging with AI. 🔴 **But 88% of
faculty report only "minimal" to "moderate" engagement, and faculty adoption is LOWEST in
assessment uses** — cheating detection and feedback generation. UNESCO IESALC's ~200-institution,
19-country study reads the same way: adoption is spreading while governance frameworks and
institutional strategies lag.
🔵 **So the LATAM opportunity is not "introduce AI" — adoption already happened, broad and
shallow.** The unserved surfaces are exactly the two the faculty numbers name as weakest:
**assessment** (the regulated surface, hence `Trend 31`'s draft-and-sign architecture) and
**governance** (the 26% formal-strategy figure already on this file). Both are advisory and
platform work rather than tool rollout, and both are billable where a licence purchase is not.

🔴 **Declared gap:** no source found in this pass gives a grounded
account of *education-specific* AI rules in these countries — the regional instruments are
general-purpose AI law, not education law.

**Demand signal — the strongest of any region.** UNESCO IESALC: **87%** of 200 HEIs across
19 countries use AI in at least one area, **74%** in teaching and learning, **26%** have a
formal strategy. TALIS 2024 teacher use: Brazil 56%, Chile 55%, Colombia 53%, Costa Rica 52%.
Brazil is the regional market leader, backed by its National AI Strategy. Tecnológico de
Monterrey signed a UNESCO agreement in March 2026.

🔴 **Equity is a measurable constraint, not a talking point.** In Argentina AI use reaches
**75% in high socio-economic groups and 42% in low** — a 33-point gap inside one country.
A deployment that assumes connectivity widens it.

**Where Globant wins.** This is the region where the governance gap is largest and best
evidenced, and where Globant's delivery presence is strongest — the natural first market for
a **governed-adoption** offer: policy, audit trail and human-in-the-loop wrapped around AI
that institutions are already running informally.
[`learningequality/kolibri`](https://github.com/learningequality/kolibri) (**MIT**, offline-first)
is the answer to the equity gap and the only MIT platform on the shelf.
[`chamilo/chamilo-lms`](https://github.com/chamilo/chamilo-lms) (GPL-3.0) has deep regional
footprint — integrate over its API, do not vendor it.
